From the First to the Second Part of the Work 111
again with a black state, the second blackness that has to be turned by the
divine water into the second whiteness (twice). That leads over to the
making purple or the reddening, the second part of the second work.
Text: (9.13) They name this whiteness the rusting (al-tasdiya), and the 125
making of the rust (as-sadaJ) is making red. And they name it the mother of
colours, the mother of dyes, the mother of the gold, the mother of the natures,
the mother of gods, the glue, the origin of the dyes, the origin for every dye,
and the sea of the two vapours, the sea of wisdom, the sea of the sages, the
marble stone, the rock (andarânî)2&1 salt and the salt of houses (common salt)
without which nothing tastes good or becomes valid. (11.5)
After the second whiteness, the transition to the redness is described 126
as the appearance of the different colours.288 It is the further result of
returning the extracted divine water, which originated from the first body,
to the second body. On the tablet of Ibn Umail’s great vision we can see
this part of the work on the left half of his image (fig. 15).
The «sea of the two vapours» is another name for the second body 127
or the white earth, the two vapours being the moist spirit and the dry
soul.289 Coming out of the unconscious, the united soul and spirit become
the symbolic insight that is the vivifying divine water. This then has to be
returned to the lower world, the dead body that is «the sea of the sages»,
being another symbol for the reality of the purified psyche of the adept.
The soaking of the second body has to take place with this divine water that
has to be first divided into nine parts. Three parts are for the lunar work
that leads first to the second blackness, and then to the second and third
whiteness. With the other six parts of the water, the solar work brings out
the colours of the second body. That is the reason why Ibn Umail calls this
white second body «the mother of colours, dyes, gold, natures and gods».290
And, as he states in al-Mä3 al-waraql, all these names are the magnesia,291
zF A pure rock salt, see A. Siggel, Arabisch-deutsches Wörterbuch der Stoffe, p. 77.
We find an identical passage on the meaning of the rusting and the rust in al-M& al-
waraqï, p. 76.21-22. This difference between the rusting and the rust is already found in the
Sfushaf as-suwar of Zosimos, fol. 57b.4-6.
Al-MäJ al-waraqï, p. 46.3 and 85.23; in the later Lamsprinck picture-series we find this
39
sea and the two vapours represented as the two fishes, swimming in the sea. Musaeum
Hermeticum, 1678, p. 343.
Al-Mä7 al-waraqï, p. 84.21-22. (See also the colour of the scorpions in fig. 51.)
30
Ibid., p. 82.12-13.
31

[Page 113]
112 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
that single arcane substance that is the origin and the goal of the work, the
finally unified individual human being.
The stone, among others, is here also called the «mother of gods»,
pointing to the emerging of many divine aspects from this white stone. In
the Mushaf as-suwar of Zosimos, which had a strong influence on Ibn
Umail, we find a passage which clarifies the meaning of this statement. In
this book, his student Theosebeia asks him: «Tell me about when the sage
says that nothing among the bodies is more virtuous than the sun. And a
pure [= complete] sun can only be the sun with its shadow.» He answered:
«The sage was right and true. And this does not merit your asking about it,
because the sun is the reddish one, and its shadow is the stone which is not
a stone.»292 This shows that the stone is the feminine aspect of consciousness
(= the sun) that brings the archetypal world to life, which by nature lies in
the shadow of consciousness. It is the shadow of monotheism, as in such
belief systems the realm of the reality of the lunar world of the archetypes
has a tendency to fade into the unconscious. But as Zosimos states clearly:
A pure [= complete] sun cannot be without its shadow.
In our text, we find then two different salts. While «the salt of houses»
or the common salt points to its general availability, «the rock salt»
(andarânï salt) needs some amplification. In an Arabic text called Al-qaul
fi ’l-milh, which might go back to the Persian physician Ibn Zakariyâ ar-
Râzï (860-925), the author speaks of the andarânï salt as the most noble of
all salts.293 There the author describes the nature of the salt and its signifi­
cance in the work, and he praises it because it is said «to contain the secret,
and whoever is able to extract and to solidify the salt has found the hidden
secret. It is the soap of the sages (säbün al-hukamä9). [...] Therefore you
have to focus on the salt, and care only about that. And you see that all the
sages striving to complete the work stay with the salt, calling it by many
names and they do not focus on anything else. [...] Whoever does not know
its secret is like a man who wants to shoot with a bow having no string.»294
We learn here that, with its sharp and fiery bitterness, the salt is able to
Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 31a.l3-16: «Therefore Democritus mentions it at the time of
292
dissolving the ivory and the stones. And one of the people named it the transformer of the
sun, and the one who named it like that was right. Whoever wants to dissolve anything
without her is making a mistake. And nothing is more honourable than this female. Therefore
they named it the good childbearer.»
293 See J. Ruska, Das Buch der Alaune und Salze, p. 51.
294 Ibid., p. 122 f.

[Page 114]
From the First to the Second Part of the Work 113
purge all futilities away like purifying soap. As Ibn Umail speaks with great
respect of ar-Razi295 this text might have been a source for his high esteem
of salt. In his al-Mä: al-waraqï salt symbolizes the successful union of spirit
and soul.296 Indeed, the sharp, burning taste of salt suggests that there must
be fire contained inside this substance.297 This union of the two is the basis
of what is described in this passage of our text, where we have a description
of the stone of the sages in its final stage of the reddening, with which
comes the return to life after the death of the first body. After the successful
union of the fiery soul (as smoke) with the watery spirit (as vapour) up in
the air this, now called divine water, comes back and unites with the second
body. This is the origin of all colours and life, the real salt of life.
Text: (11.6) And they name it frog, because it is from the water and i30
because of its way of existing and submersion back in the water from
« hich it came and with which it gets operated. And they name it the snail
of the sea, the snail of the mountains and the chameleon when it becomes
coloured by the soakings to which it is exposed, colour after colour at the
time of the making red. And [they name it] by many colours, and the
peacock, because of its multitude of colours, and [they name it] the etesian
stone (atlaïsüs), whose explanation is the multicoloured. And they name it
the son of the year because it gets born every year and it changes every two
months from one colour to another in the course of every degree of the
operation. (11.15)
All the names for the stone in this passage characterize its ability to i3i
change from one colour to another, and all the symbols are living beings:
the frog, the snail, the chameleon, the peacock and finally the son of the
year. Ibn Umail now describes the solar work, the second part of the second
w ork. The names point to the effects of the making red. This is the time
when the colours appear, when life is coming back to the dead body, the
holy thirsty earth, or the sediment. As already mentioned, and as we will
read also later in our text,298 there are now six soakings of the second body
with the divine water. These six soakings change the quality of the second
body, symbolized by the six colours of the «son of the year», one colour
for every two months.
Ad-Durra an-naqîya, fol. 13.
^ Al-MäD al-waraqï, p. 15.6 and 25.3.
yr See C. G. Jung, Myst. coni., § 313 and 320.
is* Hall ar-rumüz, p. 25.6.

[Page 115]
114 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
132 In this state, the stone is first named a frog (dufdcä). As Ibn Umail
points out, the frog’s life is closely linked to water. It is born in water, lives
first as a tadpole like a little fish in water, and then, undergoing a meta­
morphosis, it becomes a frog that can move onto the land. Yet it continues
to live close to the water. Its life shows in a nutshell what happened in the
process of evolution, when animals emerged from the water and started to
conquer the dry element. This close relationship with the watery element
connects the frog to the lunar or feminine, watery realm. The symbol of the
frog for the stone of the sages can be traced back to Zosimos, who lived in
the 3rd/4th cent. AD and came from Akhmim in Upper Egypt. In the
Mushaf as-suwar, a book attributed to him, he speaks of a frog-stone,
because it changes from one colour to another.299 He explains that this
change of colour is an expression of different states of the stone that is a
living being.300 301 The frog-stone is for Zosimos obviously a symbol of the
living stone with its different colours that appear on it during the reddening,
at the time of its reviving. At the time of Zosimos, the Pharaonic temples
Fig. 37: Osiris on the bier. Engraving in the Osiris chamber in the Ptolemaeic temple
of Dendera in Upper Egypt showing Heket, the goddess of childbirth, as a frog.301
299 Zosimos, Mushaf as-suwar,fol. 113a. 16; also in the MafatTh as-san ca(The Keys to the
Work which gives Zosimos’ commentary on a book attributed to Democritus) we find the
froggy chrysocolla (fol. 69b) and the froggy gold (fol.79b).
Mushaf as-suwar,fol. 113a. 17-19.
300
301 From Th. Flopfner, Plutarch über Isis und Osiris,Part I, p. 82.

[Page 116]
From the First to the Second Part of the Work 115
and religious cults were still alive. Horapollo (3rd cent.) reports that people
in Egypt thought that frogs were created out of the mud at the time of the
receding of the water after the annual flooding of the Nile.302 Elisabeth
Staehelin states that this belief must be based on the observation that after
the receding of the water from the Nile flood, masses of little frogs could
suddenly be found in the wet mud.303 (The hieroglyphic sign for 100,000 is
a tadpole.) Thus the Pharaonic goddess of childbirth, Heket, had the shape
of a woman with a frog head, or she was just represented by a frog. In the
latter form she is found sitting at the feet of a mummy, awaiting the revival
and rebirth of the dead body (fig. 37). All this enables us to understand that
for the Ancient Egyptians the frog became a symbol for that mysterious
force that is able to generate itself out of itself. We can assume that the
frog-symbolism of Zosimos originates in these thoughts from Pharaonic
culture. In alchemy, the frog-stone alludes to the consolidated stone that
comes to life in the unconscious of a human being, then appears in time on
the land, and is therefore a symbol for consciousness. It is the resurrection
body that can be discovered and completed by the alchemical work even
during one’s lifetime. This mysterious death and resurrection of a living
person can be traced back to the earliest Pharaonic books of the afterlife:
In the Amduat (around 1500 BC) we read repeatedly that the knowledge of
this book is not only for the deceased but that it is also «good for a person
on earth, a million times proven.»304 In Arabic texts, the frog continued to
remain a mysterious animal for its assumed generatio spontanea?05
The next symbol for the stone is the snail of the sea (haliziin al-
bahri). This symbol also seems to go back to earlier times. In the same
Mushaf as-suwar, attributed to Zosimos, he mentions the frog as well as
the snail. In various passages he says that the snail has to enter the operation
as a substance, and in one place it is one of the six dyes for the process of
reddening that takes place at the time of reviving the dead body.306 The
symbolism of the «snail of the sea» seems therefore to refer to the cowrie
snail (concha Veneris), which played a well-known role in the Pharaonic
: >: The Hieroglyphics of Horapollo, translated by G. Boas, p. 59.
; >3 In E. Hornung und E. Staehelin, Scarahäen und andere Siegelamulette aus Basler
Sammlungen I. p. 112.
.'04 The Egyptian Amduat, The Book of the Hidden Chamber, Ed. by E. Homung and Th. Abt,
e.g. p. 51 and s.v. remedy.
305 See M. Ullmann, Natur- und Geheiinwiss. im Islam, p. 54 f.
306 Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 94b.3-4.

[Page 117]
116 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
death and resurrection cult. This cowrie snail is found in the Red Sea and
it has been discovered in tombs from pre-dynastic times onwards throughout
Pharaonic history. Resembling the female sexual organ, the cowroid
became a symbol for the creative regenerative power of the feminine and
points, like the frog, to the rebirth after death.307 Already in Pharaonic
times, the cowrie snail was a symbol for the creative regenerative power of
the feminine (see fig. 38).
Fig. 38: Girdle of Mereret made of cowrie snails. It was worn by the deceased on
the hips, directly on the corpse. In Pharaonic times, the cowrie snail was a symbol
for the creative regenerative power of the feminine. (The Egyptian Museum, Cairo)
What Ibn Umail most probably meant by the snail of the mountain
(halizim al-gibäli) was the common house snail,308 as in his explanation of
the symbol he mentions its round house. In his al-McV al-waraql, Ibn
Umail tells us in detail why the snail symbolizes the stone: «I will explain
to you what the sages meant by calling their pure silvery water the water
of the snail. There are three aspects of this analogy. The first of these is
that the snail is white and round. In the same way the stone of the sages is
white and round and rolls like a pearl when it moves. [...] The second
307 E. Staehelin in: E. Hornung und E. Staehelin, Scarabäen, p. 121-122.
70s As we have only dry desert mountains in Egypt, this word must be taken symbolically.

[Page 118]
From the First to the Second Part of the Work 117
aspect of the sages' comparison of the stone to the water of the snail is that
when a snail is crawling in the mountain, its movement leaves a gleaming,
white, thin water from its moisture all along its path. The water has a
shining glitter which gleams in the sun. So they compare their own water with
it because of the whiteness of their pure, silvery water, its gleam, purity,
shininess and lustre. They say water of a snail as an analogy for their water.
The third aspect of the sages’ comparison of it with the water of the snail
is that a snail leaves its dry, thick body. It stretches out and moves, pulling
its thick body behind it with its tail wherever it crawls. Then it returns and
re-enters the body, disappearing into it so that it is invisible. And the outer
appearance of the snail is a dry, white stone, and in its interior it is a moist,
white, moving spirit. In the same way, the dry body of the sages has a soul,
spirit, life and an ability to grow. Likewise it is said about the water snail:
It is created out of the water in the water. It is in the water, and it does not
leave it, for its life is in the water and from the water.[...] In the same way,
the water of the stone of the sages comes out of their dry, coagulated, solid,
white body, just as the snail of the mountains comes out, by rotting, dis­
solution and distillation. Then their stone returns to the body, and enters it, at
the end of the operation, so that only its action and its body is seen, namely
the appearance of dyes and colours.»308 The snail must have been an
especially dear symbol for Ibn Umail because of its capacity to withdraw
completely into a protective hard shell, a mirror of his own introverted
lifestyle. The snail is, as he explains clearly, a symbol for the stone of
the sages that is an image of the sage himself. The silvery water (Arabic
= al-mä3 al-waraqT) which the snail leaves behind when it crawls gave
the first part of the title of the poem and explanation of his great vision.
The house of «the snail of the mountain» is a spiral, which in general
mythology carries the meaning of a development and resurrection that is all
the time gravitating around the centre of its origin. Thus, the spiral of the
snail points psychologically to that inner process which C. G. Jung called
individuation. Also the life of these house snails is, as for frogs, closely
related to the water and hence to the feminine priciple. The symbol of the
house snail, together with the two birds of Ibn Umail’s great vision, remained
alive in later Latin alchemy, as can be seen in figure 39 on the next page.
-«s See al-Mä3 al-waraqT, 73.28-74.18; in the edition the same passage is found earlier as well
(p. 47.8-19). However, this is a scribal error, as the older manuscripts only have the passage
once. The slow motion of the snail of the mountain does not seem to be important to Ibn Umail.

[Page 119]
118 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
Fig. 39: The snail and the two birds from Abraham Lambsprinck, Musaeum
Hermeticum, Frankfurt 1687.
The next name for the stone is the chameleon (harbäy). This animal
has the astounding capacity to change from one colour to another. This
changing of colour is done, not only to merge into the background in order
to become less noticable to enemies, but more as an expression of moods.
A chameleon shows its anger, joy or fear in a specific colour, and also
changes its colour in the course of the day. It is thus readily understandable
that the chameleon can be another name for the stone; it symbolises the
stone at the time of the solar work when the second body changes its colour
at each soaking with the six (of the nine) parts of the water which were kept
for the reddening. Later on in our text the chameleon is equated with the
thirsty holy earth, the white dead body that receives these six soakings and

[Page 120]
From the First to the Second Part of the Work 119
changes through the six colours of the rainbow, i.e. the full spectrum of
colours.310 In the same way as the chameleon changes its colours by itself,
without adding anything, the stone operates itself by itself, as the eternal
water of the 3 + 6 soakings comes out of itself. In Ibn Umail’s al-McP al-
waraql we are given some further explanation: «Then (at the time of the
solar work) that single colour, which appeared by increasing [the three
soakings in the lunar work], is followed by various colours, like the colours
of the rainbow and the colours of the peacock. Thus the earth is coloured
in the same way as the chameleon [...] because of its changing colours.
Then it returns to become deeper in the colour which first appeared from
it, and the rest of the colours become inactive. And it happens to it in this
way in every soaking that is given to it, until the completeness of the six
parts, which are the flowers, meaning the colours which appear in the earth
from their rain. And these colours, which appear in their earth in every
soaking, they name «the sick colours». Thus when these colours become
inactive, the body takes on an everlasting healthy colour - the colour of
eternal life, the purple redness. And because their stone has many colours
they called it the etesian, meaning the multi-coloured».311 Psychologically,
this play of colours heralds the return of life after a state of death, as a result
of the first part of the work. «The sick colours» then represent different
dominating emotions such as love, anger, greed, etc., that pull the ego of
the adept in different directions. But when the circle of the six colours of
the rainbow is complete «these different colours become inactive», as we
have just read. This means that one emotion or the other no longer dominates,
and the wholeness of a personality becomes balanced, «and the [second]
body takes on [one] everlasting healthy colour of eternal life, the purple
redness».312 For Ibn Umail, the alchemical work leads to the death of the
first, old body and aims at the creation of a second body, the resurrection
body that is no longer affected by decay. This points to the creation of a
stable inner wholeness that is no longer dominated and pulled in different
directions by single constellated archetypes. Like the frog and the snail,
also the chameleon is a symbol for the stone that goes back to Greek
alchemy.313
Hall ar-rumüz, p. 83.3.
310
311 Al-Mä: al-waraqî, p. 60.8-16. Concerning the many colours of the etesian stone, see in
our text p. 59.8-9.
312 Al-Mä> al-waraqî, p. 60.15.
313 E. O. von Lippmann, Entstehung I, e. g. p. 36.

[Page 121]
120 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
36 The peacock (tä^üs) is another symbol for this state of the operation.
In al-MäJ al-waraqT, Ibn Umail explains: «And Marqunis also said in
another treatise concerning the peacock: “In the inside of this white bird
there is a red bird. By the white bird he means the water which is the moist
spirit and it is the vapour. And by the red bird which is inside the white one,
he means the dye (= the different colours) which is the soul about which I
told you that it is the effective power.”»314 The white peacock emphasises
its symbolic nature. It is a container that unites the different colours. As a
symbol it shows that the goal of the work is attained when the different
emotional impulses are replaced by a colourless constancy that is able to
contain the fiery red bird that is the soul, from whence emerge the different
colours, the colourful splendour of life. This is like a peacock which displays
its feathered splendour, but can also make it disappear again.
37 The son of the year (ibn as-sana) is a name that Ibn Umail explains
later in our text as being «the son of the six bodies, which are the six parts
isolated from the water for the reddening». And there he also states that «they
named it the water of the 12 signs of the zodiac.»315 The name «son of the
year» also goes back to Greek alchemy, where it was called the etesian stone
( lithos etêsios),316 Here it is named atlaïsüs. In his Mushaf as-suwar, Zosimos
states that «with regard to the stone’s true name, it is the stone which is bom
every year. This is because it has in it the nature of the truth and, God the
Exalted willing, the moon gives birth to it every year, and it is burnt. It is the
monthly courses of the moon which give it its place in that zodiac sign.»317
?m Al-Mä: al-waraqï, p. 99. 12-14.
.-15
Hall ar-rumüz, p. 67.10-11 and p. 67.6.

su Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 218a.6-8; in the Mafatïh as-sanca Zosimos speaks in almost the
same way as Ibn Umail of the etesian stone: «Thus I asked: “Why did the sages name the
composition etesian?” He replied: “It is because they declared that the etesian is born every
year and it has different colours that change every month from one colour to another.
Therefore they named their composition etesian stone, because it changes in every degree of
the operation from one colour to another every month.”» (fol.72a) And later he says: « And
when this etesian stone is operated on, a new child will come out of it, in the same way as it
comes out of the male and the female. Do not be amazed that it becomes one stone from
various things and various stones. [...] Just like that is our golden stone, which we named
etesian. It comes out of various things. Thus the dyeing makes different dyes come out of it,
until it becomes like the complete dye, which does not change if you try it out, and it is
completed, (fol. 83a)

[Page 122]
From the First to the Second Part of the Work 121
This quote shows that the creation of the stone is not a one-off affair but
needs the lifelong care of the adept in order that out of the unconscious
(symbolized by the moon), consciousness (symbolized by the sun), is
regularly renewed. This revolving rejuvenation of consciousness by the
lunar world expresses the changes in the realm of the unconscious in the
best way. This is, after all, the explanation of why Christmas is celebrated.
The birth of Christ, who in the early days was called Sol Novus (new sun),318
is also an expression of the experience that the image of the divine needs
regular renewal, as the sun renews itself daily and in the course of the year.
The six waters that remain for the solar work change the stone its
«every two months from one colour to another in the course of every degree
of the operation» in order to complete the work. The number six is related
to the six colours of the rainbow, as Ibn Umail mentioned in the passage on
Fig. 40: The six colours of the rainbow related to the circle of the year, the warm
colours belonging to the day, the cold ones belonging to the night. (From Th. Abt,
Picture Interpretation, p. 87.)
the chameleon earlier. But it also relates to the full circle of transformation
in time (6 x 2 = 12 months), as the number six is the natural division of the
The Roman emperor Aurelian introduced 274 AD the Sol Invictus (the unconquerable
sun) as the highest God of the Roman empire, reducing by that decree the divine nature of the
Caesar to a ‘'ruler on earth” for the highest god.

[Page 123]
122 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
2nd double month 3rd double month
(Rabr I (Jumada I
Rabi1 II) Jumada II)
month
(Ramadan. Shauwal)
Fig. 41: The six colours of the rainbow related to the circle of the Islamic lunar
year. Originally the names of the months appeared in pairs, still visible in the names
of the second and the third double month.
course of time, the circle containing six times the radius. Originally the
Islamic year was divided into double months, which explains the change of
colour every two months. The son of the year that is bom every year is, of
course, the sun. Its rebirth was always highly venerated by humans in the
same way as the sunrise after the dark night. This shows that the stone is
an experience that needs to be renewed repeatedly. Consciousness has, by
its very function, to be stable and solid in order to function well. For that
reason, the art of the renewal of consciousness has always preoccupied
humans, as can be seen, for instance, in the Royal Books of the Afterlife in
Ancient Egypt.319 The two diagrams (fig. 40 and 41) show that the stone
unifies in itself all the emotional qualities of the colours. At the same time
it unites all the qualities of time, symbolized in the astrological months, the
course of the sun through the zodiac.
319 See e.g. Th. Abt and E. Hornung, Knowledge for the Afterlife—A Quest for Immortality.

[Page 124]
123
5. The Eros-quality of the Stone
Text: (11.16) And they named it “every thing’’ because everything 139
they need in their art is in it, and from it, and by it, and for it. All of that is
in it, and from it, and by it only because it does not need anything else, and
nothing that is not from it enters into it. That is because if something alien
were to enter into it. this would corrupt it and paralyse it, and it would take
away its strength and its activity. (13.3)
The name «every thing» is mentioned later on in our text in different 140
places. In one place, for instance, Ibn Umail mentions that they named
mercury and magnesia «every thing».320 In the al-MâJ al-waraqî, Ibn
Umail calls the full moon «the origin of every thing», because out of it
come the soul and the spirit. There he equates the full moon with the
worked-upon first body, which symbolizes the individual at the beginning
of the work.321 In another place in this same book, Ibn Umail says that
«they gave their stone the name of every thing in the world.»322 And earlier
he points to the result of the transforming work of this one stone—that can
be anything in this world—saying: «Therefore they named it [the stone]
“every thing”, because everything comes from it and when it resisted the
fire, it acquired a great strength.»323 In this context Ibn Umail is equating
the stone with every thing in the material world, and at the same time also
with the process as well as the goal of the work, namely the one uniting
stone that is resistant to the fire. This points to the transformed fire (which
he calls the soul) that became tamed or contained by the watery spirit. In
Hall ar-rumüz, p. 31.16-33.1.
320
321 Al-M5}al-waraql, p. 45.15-17.
Ibid., p. 90.15.
322
323 Ibid., p. 81.22-23.

[Page 125]
124 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
the second part of the work, this unified soul and spirit, which came out of
the full moon, is called «the divine water» or «the dye». This water is
brought back to the second body in nine parts or soakings. According to Ibn
Umail, the dye is also called «every thing», as is the second body that
receives and collects «every thing» (= the dye).324 In al-Mä: al-waraqï, the
name for the stone is also «every body». 325 This name for the stone is also
found in the Arabic texts of Zosimos.326
141 Psychologically, every thing and every body in this material world
can arouse the fire of passion and can, in this way, lead to the experience
of the mystery of the stone. That somebody or something in the bodily
world can become irresistibly attractive has always been a mystery and was
feared, because it «demonstrates quite plainly our secret inferiority».327
«Every thing» and «every body» can contain the mystery of attraction of
the bodily world. That demonstrates the divine power of creation. The
secret of the overpowering fascination comes from the fact that behind this
attraction of matter lies the hidden wholeness of Mother Nature, the feminine
side of the divinity. Connecting consciousness to that unconscious totality
leads to the renewed experience of the whole individual. Ibn Umail points
in our text to this magic attraction of the divine, seen mirrored in creation.
It is the origin, as he says: «all is from it». But this is also the force that main­
tains the process, so «all is by it», and it is the goal of the work, so «all is for
it». The individual with his or her unique body and drive-structure is the
secret of the work—it is really «every thing», and the goal of «every thing»,
and nothing needs to be added to the process for the individual to develop.
142 Text: (13.3) And they named it a staff of Moses because the staff of
Moses—may there be peace on him—had a spirit in it, and it became a
324 In al-Mâ: al-waraqï, p. 80.1-3., Ibn Umail explains what he means by «every thing»,
quoting the learned alchemist Aras, who told the king Theodoros «that they call the stone
“every thing” because everything that they need in their work is collected in it.» When the
King asked: «And what thing is [this] “every thing”?» Aras replied: «It is the dye of the body.»
325 E.g.: «And they named that body after “every body”. And they also named their water
after “every body”, because it came out from that body which they named after “every body”
of what people know [...].». Al-Mâ} al-waraqï, p. 56.13-15. See also p. 38.23 and 81.20-23.
326 For instance in the Mushaf as-suwar, on fol. 27a. 10, 36a.4, and 55a. 12.
327 C. G. Jung, Myst. coni. § 152. Because this feminine force of attraction is first experienced
as an overpowering force, this has been considered by Book religions such as Judaism,
Christianity and Islam as being opposed to the ruling principle. The feminine principle
therefore became associated with evil.

[Page 126]
The Eros-quality of the Stone 125
dragon that swallowed all that was thrown to it. Likewise their stone is a
spirit that swallows all the nourishment and soakings, which are given to
it. And it is the dragon and from it comes the head of the dragon that is its
water, which goes out from it and with it and in it. (13.8)
The staff of Moses as a name for the stone is connected to the snake
143
miracle of the staff of Moses as told in the Bible.328 A shortened version of
this miracle is also reported in the Koran.329 In the Bible story, which was
also known in the Islamic world, we read that Moses was ordered by God
to have his brother Aaron perform a miracle for the Pharaoh by throwing
his staff in front of him. It turned into a snake. Thus challenged, the
Pharaoh called on his sages and priests to do the same with their staffs,
namely miraculously to transform non-living matter into living snakes.
And they could perform this miracle as well. But the staff of Moses, used
by Aaron, was then able to swallow all these snakes of the magicians. Later
in our text, Ibn Umail calls this staff of Moses a «dragon with nine
heads».330 And from Ibn Umail's commentary to his al-Qasida an-nünïya
we learn «that they [the earlier sages] named the moist spirit a staff, because
the staff of Moses [...] turned into a moving spirit, a dragon, that swallowed
all that was thrown at it, making it disappear into its belly.»331 The staff of
Moses is therefore for Ibn Umail a symbol for the spirit that extracts the soul
from the body and is able to swallow or contain it. As there are nine
eagles332 or nine parts of the divine water, which came out of the first body,
these nine are united in this «snake with nine heads». The one unites the nine.
In the same way as the staff of Moses unites the dissociated manifoldness
of the snakes of the magicians into one, the stone of the sages, which is the
second body, condenses all the nine soakings with the divine water into one,
uniting the different emotional aspects (colours) of the individual.
The stone is then called the dragon, «and from it comes the head of
144
the dragon, that is its water, which goes out from it and with it and in it.»
The head of the dragon is the watery spirit that comes out of its fiery body
with its dangerous, strong emotional urges or cravings. This water carries
328 Exodus, 7.1-12.
Sura 20.55-7; according to the Bible and the Koran, Moses was able to communicate
329
directly with God.
Hall ar-rumüz, p. 77.7.
330
Al-QasTda an-nünïya, fol. 133b.11-14.
331
This is described in Ibn Umail's great vision in al-MäJ al-waraqi, for example on p. 1.6.
332

[Page 127]
126 Commentary on the Hall ar-nmuz
the essence of the fiery dragon in the form of images «with it», and it is
returned «in it», back to the origin, the dragon, or what Ibn Umail calls the
second body.333 334
Psychologically, this water from the head refers to the subtle part of
the fiery emotion that was extracted or distilled out of the first body. The
water that comes out of the head of the dragon is the divine water, the
union of soul and spirit that will then, in nine soakings, be reunited with the
second body. The whole process takes place within the dragon, which is
described in Greek alchemy as the ouroboros, the tail-biting snake or dragon,
well known as a symbol for cyclical renewal and rejuvenation since the
New Kingdom of Ancient Egypt. This divine water, as the evolutionary
libido of the collective unconscious, circulates in itself and renews itself
and is the basis of the creation of consciousness in the individual and on
the collective level.

6th hour. The Amduat is the first Royal Book of the Afterlife from the New
Kingdom, dating around 1500 BC.334
For a more practical understanding of this, see C. G. Jung, Memories, Dreams, Reflections,
333
p. 177 and 187.
The Egyptian Amduat, The Book of the Hidden Chamber, edited by E. Hornung and
334
Th. Abt, p. 200. See Als Th. Abt and E. Hornung, Knowledge for the Afterlife, p. 83 f.

[Page 128]
The Eros-quality of the Stone 127
Fig. 43: The ouroboros (Greek = tailbiter) with four legs how it is later represented
in a Greek alchemical manuscript of Synesius. (15th cent. Bibi. Nationale Paris)
Text: (13.8) And it is its sediment, and the sun, and the fiery body. It is i46
[also] the moon, the furnace, the white sulphur, the red sulphur, the sulphurs and
the red sulphur. They name it with all of this when it is white. (13.11)
Here we see again, as at the beginning with the symbol of lead- 147
copper, that the stone can only be described as a paradox, consisting of all
kinds of opposites. It is the sediment and the furnace, meaning the contained
and the container, it is the sun and the moon, and finally it is the female
white sulphur and the male red sulphur. The stone is the soul and the spirit,
yet at the same time it is also the body, from which all originates. It is
obvious that with these symbols Ibn Umail tries to point to a wholeness
that words can never express.
The different sulphurs need some detailed reflection. Sulphur 148
(kibrït) is found in nature as a hard, inflammable yellow rock. The word
sulphur is supposed to have derived from a Latin term sulphur, meaning

[Page 129]
128 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
Fig. 44: Sulphur as yellow rock. Sulphur is found in nature as hard yellow rock that
bums. The word sulphur is supposed to have derived from a Latin term sulphur
meaning “burning stone” whose equivalent in English is brimstone.
“burning stone” whose equivalent in English is brimstone.335 It was already
known in ancient times in Mesopotamia and Egypt. In Greece it is
described by Aristotle, who named it divine, pointing to the different
colours that appear on it when heated. Pure sulphur was used in antiquity for
religious purposes, for disinfection, bleaching of textiles, and as an externally
applied remedy.336 Edward Edinger has also pointed out some prominent
features in the chemistry of sulphur. Hydrogen sulphide, for instance,
makes up the unpleasant smell of rotten eggs and is the main constituent of
flatus. Sulphur dioxide is the main cause of acid rain and both hydrogen
sulphide and sulphur dioxide have a corroding effect on many metals.
Sulphur is also the central ingredient of gunpowder.337 In earlier days we
had sulphur directly on matchsticks that could therefore easily ignite, even
when damp. Because of this risk of self-ignition we have so-called safety
matches today. In a negative, dangerous sense, sulphur is associated with the
devil, hellfire and the devil’s smell, while in the positive sense it is a symbol
335 See in detail: E. F. Edinger, The Mysterium Lectures, p. 95. For sulphur in general, see
C. G. Jung, Myst. coni., Chapter on Sulphur, especially § 151.
336 J. Weyer in: C. Priesner and K. Figala, Alchemielexikon, p. 327.
337 E. F. Edinger, ibid., p. 95 f.

[Page 130]
The Eros-quality of the Stone 129
for the light- and warmth-bringer Lucifer (lux ferens). Sulphur has these
two sides, being a symbol for the destructive and the culture bringing fire.
Ibn Umail mentions here in our text the white sulphur, the red sulphur
with a feminine ending, sulphurs, and then red sulphur with the normal,
masculine ending. Later in our text he says that the red sulphur is the fiery
Fig. 45: Sol-sulphur. Sulphur is associated with the fiery sun (sol). The transfor­
mation of sol-sulphur, the ‘devilish fire of desirousness’, played a central role in
alchemy. This picture is found in a later German manuscript. Sol-sulphur stands on
the tablet that is known from the great vision of Ibn Umail, showing Ibn Umail’s
strong influence on the symbolic-religious branch of later Latin alchemy.
(C. Hartung, Das kleine Kunstbüchlein, 1549)

[Page 131]
130 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
soul 338 that, when solidified and dried, is named white or whitened
sulphur.339 In al-MaJ al-waraqT he is more explicit, saying: «The white
sulphur is the spirit in which lies the soul; with it [the white sulphur] it [the
soul] is extracted from its body. And it [the soul] is the red sulphur.»340 And
in the same book he states that «they named also their body sulphur and
sulphurs and the red sulphur which is the soul. But they distracted the
people with the sulphurs that they know, so that the people would not know
the soul which they have and which they name the red sulphur. [This red
sulphur is] carried in their water, which they named the white sulphur.
Although it is not a sulphur, it performs the action of the sulphur. Therefore
the early sages warned the people by their statement: “Our sulphurs are not
the sulphurs of the people.” This is because the sulphur of the people bums
with a black and ruining burning, and it bums and also is burnt. But the white
sulphur of the sages bums with a beneficial and white burning and all the
burning of the sages makes white.»341 From this it becomes clear that by the
different sulphurs Ibn Umail does not mean material substances. They are
symbols for psychic factors. The red sulphur (that is both male and female)
is that fiery substance of desirousness or compulsion that has to be contained
by the work. When this red sulphur is whitened and solidified by the white
sulphur it becomes the source of all colours or dyes, in the same way as
sulphur in the outer world gives wonderful brilliant colours to the painter’s
palette. In his al-Mä; al-waraqT Ibn Umail talks about this: «Thus I tell who­
ever comes after me that what made these colours appear is the composed
water, which is the pure sulphur and the eternal water with many names. And
know that I only meant by this saying of mine [what is] in the second work,
and I did not mention the first [work] at all.»342 The pure sulphur is what is
named here «sulphurs», i.e. the union of the red and the white sulphur, or of
soul and spirit. This pure sulphur is then called eternal or sulphury water,
which has to be reunited with the white silvery earth that is the second body,
the process that takes place in the second part of the work. For the further
development of the symbolism of the two sulphurs (sulphur duplex) in later
Latin alchemy I refer to C. G. Jung's chapter on sulphur.343
150 To illustrate Ibn Umails’s process, let us take the example of a
person that has some ‘dark’, i.e. incomprehensible fantasy, for instance
33 Hall ar-rumüz, p. 55.13 Ibid., p. 31.14.
s 339
Al-Mâ3 al-waraqï, p. 95.17-18. Ibid., p. 28.4-8.
340 341
Ibid., 69 13 15
342 p. . - .
In Myst. coni., § 130 f.
343

[Page 132]
The Eros-quality of the Stone 131
the compulsion to be wrapped up like a mummy. Such a compulsion usually
culminates in a passionate orgasm that brings relief and leaves the person
more or less satisfied and happy. Taken on the concrete level only, that
person will feel compelled to repeat that urge periodically. It is a compulsion
and as such an unfree state, because it is a drive or urge without any
apparent meaning. On the instinctual level there is only repetition. On the
biological level there is no modification or transformation, since the drives
are programmed for repetition. There is, however, a deeper meaning in any
urge. When such a fantasy is drawn or painted on paper (in alchemical
terms, put in a vessel) and looked at, out of this passion will come—because
of the tension created by suspending the sexual urge—an image or images
that are in fact a symbol containing a hidden meaning. In the case of our
mummification ritual, this will somehow be connected, in quite a unique
way of course, to the mystery of the inner death, purification and rejuvenation
of that individual. As we have seen, this coming together of fire (the drive)
and water (the image), or sun and moon, is experienced in the beginning as
an unfree state, an eclipse of the sun. This is because it is sensed as an
incompatible clash of two worlds, the clear-structured day-world and the
bewildering dark night-world that is the chaotic emotional background. In
the beginning the opposites are not peacefully but passionately united
(passion is connected etymologically with suffering). But if this eclipse of
the opposites can be seen in its value as prima materia, and if the craving
can be suspended for the sake of the work, then something can happen: The
images behind the emotion can become visible. This is symbolized in the
white sulphur that is the underlying spiritus veritatis, the hidden meaning of
the drivenness. This white sulphur comes out, or is being distilled out, of the
body at the same time as the fire of the drivenness is kept in suspension.
This white sulphur is, like the red sulphur, a divine active force that is able
to contain the fire. This union of the white and red sulphur is experienced
as the mysterious union of drive and drive regulation. Whether or not and
how long the wrapping up as a mummy or any other “dark fantasy” will be
meaningful as a ritual is an open question, as this process of extracting the
soul from the body is one that takes place step by step. The goal of the
alchemical work is the transformation of the meaningless, unfree state of
addiction, craving or compulsion, into something durable that changes the
quality of the stone. It changes from possessive blackness or unconscious­
ness (the first body) to healing, redeeming whiteness, and later to life-giving
redness in the second body, as Ibn Umail calls that psychic state.

132 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz

Fig. 46: Ouroboros, the tail-biter, represented here as scorpion connected to the
winged bird. This is a variant of the two connected birds in the great vision of Ibn
Umail. The lower poison-carrying scorpion (caqrab) is a symbol for the red sulphur,
the fiery soul, while the upper winged bird represents the white sulpur or the watery
spirit. The picture is a symbol for the reunited drive and drive-regulation.344
Text: (13.11) And it is the ferment of the water, the ferment of silver,
the ferment of gold, and acid ferment, lime, human excrement, dung, and
white firewood when it is desiccated and dry. It is the source of life and
whoever has drunk from it will never get thirsty again. And it is the eye of
the animal, and the mountain of the sources because from it comes their
divine, silvery, pure water which revives their earth and everything on it.
(13.16)
From the Kitäb al-Aqâlïm as-sabaca of Abü al-Qäsim al-cIräql (British Libr., London)
344

[Page 134]
The Eros-quality of the Stone 133
A small quantity of ferment (hatmr) has the quality of being able to 152
actively transform a large quantity of matter. In his al-Mâ ’ al-waraqï, Ibn
Umail explains the different ferments. They are the divine water that has
been divided up into nine parts: «The ferment of silver is the three parts of
these nine parts of the [divine] water, with which they make black and
[then] make white. And the remaining six [parts] are the ferment of the
gold, with which they dye.»345 The acid ferment, which is also mentioned
here, points to the active influence of the divine water on the second body,
the purified psyche of the adept. Psychologically the ferments are the
vitalizing insights that emanate from the images of the unconscious. When
these insights are united or connected with the reality of life, they do
indeed become a never-ending source of life.
Limestone (gfr, CaC03) turns, when burnt, into quicklime (CaO), 153
which has the surprising quality that, when mixed with water, it generates
heat. To create a body that is able to contain the divine fire of the soul is
the main goal of the work, as was repeated innumerable times by Ibn
Umail. In the same way as the fire in the house has to be well contained in
a stove, so the inner, emotional fire of desires, compulsions, etc., has to be
prevented from spreading, and dominating the personality. Lime as a name
for the stone of the sages symbolizes the consolidated personality in its
state of being a container for the divine fire.
The fact that Ibn Umail refers to their stone as human excrement -
15
shows that the generally most despised is also the most exalted. This
becomes quite important if we think of how today in therapy we can find
that precisely what has been rejected most, left behind as awful smelling
defecation, should be seen as a form of the stone (in Latin alchemy it is
said: in stercore invenitur - it is found on the dungheap). What is often just
a ‘nothing but ...’ can turn into the gold of the sages. Lrom the totally
devalued, the highest value can be extracted by the opus.
On the subject of the symbols of human excrement and dung (zibl) 155
we can read in al-Mâ: al-waraqï a quotation from Hälid ibn Yazld stating
that what they meant by that was the body, while he names the clarified
part urine: «When they took away its clarified part, which are the souls and
the spirits [...] they named its sediment excrement and dung.»346 Human
excrement and dung is the body at the bottom of the vessel, to which the
Al-MaJ al-waraqi, p. 54.21-22; see also for example, p. 70.11-12.
345
346 Ibid., p. 96.2-3.

[Page 135]
134 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
sages return the nine parts of the water in the second part of the work. But
at another point in al-Ma:j al-waraqïhe also compares dung with the divine
water: «If the earth is not fertilized with dung, it will not be broken up, and
it will not ferment, and its plants will not increase, and its products will not
be good.»347 Dung is on the one hand the body and on the other hand the
divine water. This shows again the two aspects of the one stone, the indi­
vidual human being who contains both, the low and the high. Because of
this inner duality, a person is able to be fertilized by him- or herself, by the
insights of the divine water created out of his or her relationship with the
unconscious.
The «eye of the animal» is for Ibn Umail another symbol for the
stone. The eye is elsewhere known as a name for mercury, the spirit and
Fig. 47: The eye of a horse. The eye of the animal is also a symbol of the stone of
the sages. Such an insight changes one’s attitude towards the lower world.
Al-Mä^ al-waraqi, p. 85.19-20.
347

[Page 136]
The Eros-quality of the Stone 135
light of nature.348 It symbolizes a latent consciousness or luminosity that is
unified by the stone in the same way as the eye collects everything that it
sees.349 To look into the eye of an animal gives human beings a hint of that
preconscious totality that is longing to become conscious. To reach this
wholeness on a conscious level is the quest of the alchemist. This symbol
of the eye of the animal appeared in a dream of a 65-year-old married man
with a good Christian upbringing and a successful life. However, he was
now tortured by a passionate sexual desire for another woman. Whenever
he lived it out. he sank afterwards into despair about it, unable to understand
what was happening to him. A dream showed him an image of himself:
«... I look into the mirror and see that I have another face. First I
think this is the face of an extraterrestrial being, but then I realize that my
face has also the features of an animal. I look like a capricorn, especially
the eyes. I am very surprised to see myself still like an animal. I am not
scared. »
This dream shows that it can be one’s very drivenness that makes 157
one see something one had not previously realized sufficiently. Man's
animal nature, with all its sexual appetites, is not alien, not like something
from another planet. It is just part of common "human' animal nature that
the dreamer had lost sight of and needed to see again. This fire of unorthodox
passion is not simply evil. It is, however, as the dream shows, still inhuman
and autonomous, like a wild animal in its natural habitat. It is not yet
connected to a meaning that would enable the dreamer to contain this fire
within the framework of his whole personality. The eye of the animal is the
essence of the other side of the Christian attitude, the prima materia that
needs to be seen, and humanized by careful and patient work. Then, when
in connection with a human being, it can become the collecting symbol of
the second body.
The symbol «mountain of sources» also calls for a comment from s
15
Ibn Umail’s al-Mä; al-waraqk. There he quotes the sage Asfïdüs
(Secundus), who said: «[...]. Raise them up to their highest places and
harvest them from the tops of their mountains, in which they are found.»
Ibn Umail then explains that what is meant is «their pure divine water,
which has in it and from it and by it this wind which is the efficient power.
It is the living dyer-soul, with which the vapour got impregnated by the
348 See L. Thorndike, History of Science I, p. 760 and 766 and A. Siggel, Decknamen, p. 46.
See J. Ruska, Steinbuch des Aristoteles, p. 8, n4.
349

[Page 137]
136 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
(other) vapour (= the smoke, the soul), and it gives life to everything into
which it enters. It cannot be seen nor felt except by the science and nothing
of it can be seen except its effect, which is the appearing of the dye in their
body, and the colours which they name flowers, and flowers of the herb,
and other (names) like that for the dyes.»350 From this it becomes clear that
what Ibn Umail meant by «the mountain of sources» was the elevated and
purified aspect of the stone, which «comes from their divine silvery pure
water, which revives their earth and everything on it». This takes place at
the time of the reddening, when the second body or the thirsty earth is
soaked with the divine water, as our text sais.
159 Text: (13.16) And it is the copper and the cinnabar, and in every
cooking it takes a colour from the red (colours), until three soakings, three
cookings and three successive uprisings are completed for it. Thus it
becomes a red cinnabar. And at that time it is named cinnabar. (15.3)
160 Here Ibn Umail gives some other names to summarize the trans­
formation. Copper is the stone at the beginning state, «having a body, a
soul, and a spirit», as we have seen. But it is also the goal of the work when
it became a copper without shadow, the whitened copper. Besides copper he
mentions cinnabar (qinbär) that in its cmde form is blackish, while in its
Fig. 48: Cinnabar in its cmde form is blackish, while in its pure form it is of a
beautiful, shining red.
350 Al-Mä^ al-waraqi, p. 99.16-20.

[Page 138]
The Eros-quality of the Stone 137
pure form it is of a beautifully shining red. This ability of cinnabar to change
must have inspired the alchemists to see in it a symbol for their stone.
Cinnabar (HgS2) is also a symbol for the successful containment of the fire
of the sulphur (S) by the water or the spirit that is the other component of
the cinnabar, the flowing metal mercury (Hg). The «three soakings, three
cookings and three risings» must be other symbols for the nine soakings,
as the result of these operations is red (i.e. purified) cinnabar.
Text: (15.4) They also named it wool and firewood of the wool. i6t
And they named it the iron when the female entered in it and it resisted the
fire like the resistance of iron, because iron does not dissolve in the fire like
other things and it does not flee from it. (15.7)
The wool (süf) and the firewood of the wool (hatab as-süf) are further i62
names of the stone. A statement later in our book clarifies them: «Maria the
sage named it (the stone) the fleece of wool and the woollen dress. [...] She
(also) said: “Dye the fleece of wool." They named the water the wool and
they named the body the firewood of the wool. They named the wool the
water because it accepts the dyes and it rises upon the body, and from the
body it came out.»351 From this we see that the wool is a symbol of the
stone in its liquid form as water. But at the same time we see from this
quote that the wool is also a symbol of the second body that «accepts the
dyes» by the firewater that is containing the fiery dyer-soul. «The firewood
of the wool» is also a symbol of the stone but in its solid state. It is the body
out of which the wool grows, symbol for the union of the watery spirit with
the fiery soul.352 Firewood as a symbol for the first body is quite under­
standable: it contains the fiery soul hidden inside. While burning, this first
body gets consumed by the fire and is reduced to ashes, while spirit and
soul (symbolized by vapour and smoke) are extracted. That product is
symbolized by wool, which is another symbol for the stone as the second
body. What grew out of the first body is what can now be dyed. This,
however, is what makes understanding these alchemical texts difficult:
The names for the stone at first, for the water, and for the second body
are all interchangeable. All these names express the different stages and
experiences of the mysterious self-purification and self-amelioration of
the psyche, which takes place as a result of the work of the adept on him-
or herself with the help of the divine water that itself is also a symbol of
351 Hall ar-rumüz, p. 69.3-8.
352 The symbols for wool and firewood can already be found in the Mushaf as-suwar of
Zosimos, fol. 126b. 17-18, and on fol. 92b. 11.

[Page 139]
138 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumu:
Fig. 49: Magnetic lodestone. The lodestone with its magnetism has become a
symbol for the irresistible attraction that also humans experience in nature.
the stone. And most important of all, as Ibn Umail and his teachers always
hasten to add. this work is not possible without the help and grace of God.
163 «The iron into which the female entered», has the quality of resisting
the heat of the fire; it only turns red. By the female that entered this metal
of Mars our author must have meant magnetism: «And they named the
magnesia (i.e. the female) magnet because it attracts the iron so that it [the
iron] clings to it.»353 Through the iron’s quality of reacting easily to
magnetism, and then being able to become magnetic itself, this metal
obtains the miraculous quality of containing something that can become
active, and this gives it the feeling of being alive by being itself attractive.
By magnetism, that is the female and another name for the divine water,
the iron became complete. This union of the iron and the magnet, which is,
as we have just seen, the magnesia, refers to the union of the divine water
with the second body, that is now able to resist fire.
164 Text: (15.7) And they named it brain. Marqünis named it the brain
of the ghoul, because the thoughts are concealed in the brain and in the
same way the dye of their stone is concealed in the interior, just as thoughts
are concealed in the brain and do not appear. But what appears from the
thoughts are only its results. (15.11)
353 Hall ar-rumuz, p. 33.14-15.

[Page 140]
The Eros-quality of the Stone 139
For Ibn Umail, as we can also see from a later passage, the brain is 165
a symbol for the sages’ divine water, which contains the fire, the sulphur
dye.354 But the brain is also a symbol for the second body that was dyed in
the second part of the work and now contains the dye, the fiery soul. The
ghoul is a dangerous desert demon also mentioned in the Koran. This
demon can be understood as the destructive side of fire, which is able to
kill all life. It was already known in Pharaonic times, when, according to
the myth, the fiery red Seth, the master of the desert, killed his brother
Osiris, the master of the water. As a symbol for the stone, «the brain of the
ghoul» points to the need to contain in the brain the dangerously maddening
quality of the fire-aspect of the stone. The ghoul needs to be overcome
again and again by the watery spirit in order for it to be finally and perma­
nently contained in the vessel of the brain, the second body. Contained
inside, the fire becomes a source of energy for the personality. The symbol
of the brain-stone is also already known from Greek alchemy, as was pointed
out by C. G. Jung, who wrote on “Rotundum, Head and Brain”. There he
states that the devilish demon «can be correlated with the brain as the seat
of consciousness and intelligence, for consciousness leads an 'ungodly’
existence, having fallen away from the divine totality.»355
Text: (15.11) They also named it the vessel, the alembic, the temple i66
and the congregational mosque as it gathers together all persons of our
science. Likewise the vessel collected the mixture, in which they have all
that is in it and therefore they named this mixture—which is their stone, the
lead-copper and the magnesia—the temple. And they named it the congre­
gational mosque because it collects whatever they need of the moist, dry,
hot and cold things, the spirits and souls, ashes, bodies, ferments and dyes.
Thus, it is all, and from it is all, and in it is all. And they also named it
"every thing” and all of these are from the unknown familiar one. And they
named it the receiver at the time of the distillation and the descending of
the w'ater to it, for the reunion of the spirits. (17.7)
The reason the stone is also compared to a temple is given in detail 167
in al-MäJ al-waraqï, where Ibn Umail says: «By the birbä (Egyptian
temple) [...] he meant the second body, which is their white earth in which
they sow their gold, and their gold is the soul. And that (second) body,
Hall ar-rumüz, p. 53.3.
354
355 C. G. Jung, Myst. coni., § 626, referring to M. Berthelot, Coll. Alch. Grecs I.I11.1 and
III.II.l.

[Page 141]
140 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
which is the object of the science and the collector of it and the place of the
dyes, is compared with the temple of their sciences, which is the collector
of the sciences of the philosophy. This is their body in which they coagulate
that water. And in saying “it lights up”, he means the appearing of the light
of the dye in it. [...] And the temple in another place is the retort and the
alembic, which is the collector of all of their sciences. And the sages had
manifold ways of making comparisons».356 It is clear that by all the different
symbols of this passage, such as temple, mosque, etc, Ibn Umail refers to
the second or subtle body with its quality of being able to unite the different
parts of the psyche. The symbols of the houses of God, where the community
gathers like the different parts of the individual, represents the uniting
power of the love for the art that is the love for God. This eros-quality of
the stone unites the individual in the first place, but also brings together the
people who are striving for the same goal. The common respect for the
stone thus can become the centre of a common house of God in the global
village.
168 Text: (17.8) Muhammad ibn Umail. may God have mercy on him,
said: «The extraction of such meanings and their precise expressions from
their books is extremely difficult for people. But nobody is able to reveal
the secrets of people who have died and perished with their secrets, hidden
in their bygone consciences and their decayed breasts, except the one
whose mind God opens and whom he supports with His spirit.» (17.12)
169 On two occasions in al-Mcû al-waraqï we find similar quotations.357
They could originally have been the marginal glosses of a student of Ibn
Umail and later been taken up in the main text.
356 Al-Ma> al-waraqi, p. 33.19-26; see also p. 37.9 and 100.25.
357 Ibid., p. 78.17 and 102.8.

[Page 142]
141
6. The Dyer and the Dyed
Text: (17.13) They named their stone—which is the magnesia— no
gold and they said: «Turn the gold into dyeing poison.» And they also said:
«Whoever is able to turn the gold into a dyeing poison has found the right
way, and whoever found the true way has found it. But whoever is not able
to turn it into a killing poison is nowhere.» And they also said about this
magnesia: «0. all you seekers of this science, if you want to cure your
souls from the disease of poverty, all seductions, and sorrows then dye the
gold red. For if you dye it red it will hold the dye, and it gets dyed. God the
Exalted willing. (19.6)
The dyeing of the second body plays a central role in the alchemy m
of Ibn Umail. To understand its symbolism a visit to his probable home­
land. Egypt, is helpful. At the workplace of the dyer, located in the souk,
we can get a feeling for what it means to prepare the textiles and the
process of dyeing. Most elements of the process have not changed over the
centuries. Even the place where the textiles are dyed still reminds one of a
Pharaonic sarcophagus, as can be seen from figure 50 on the next page.
The process of dyeing was already of great importance in Greek 172
alchemy but its origin goes back to Pharaonic times. According to texts from
the 19th Dynasty (1291-1098 BC), the dyers had their workplace partly in
the temples. From that time on there are documents speaking of the pro­
fession of the dyer (ps-jns).35i In alchemy, the work of the dyer became an
important symbol for the goal of the work. Here in our text we now have
magnesia in the form of the divine water that is also named gold. And this
gold has to be turned «into a dyeing poison» which both dyes and kills.
This is explained later in the Hall ar-rumüz when Ibn Umail writes that
See R. Germer, Die Textilfärberei und die Verwendung gefärbter Texilien im Alten
358
Ägypten, p. 134 and 137.

[Page 143]
142 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
Fig. 50: The workplace of a dyer in Old Cairo in 1998. The way textiles are dyed,
as well as the equipment used, has not changed much over the centuries.
«the spirit, which is the dyeing water for the soul, is [also] dyed by it. At that
point they become one dye. And they named it fire, dyeing poison and fiery
poison and it is the flying eagle .. ,».359 Later on he states that they named it
«the sharp sword which is the killing poison, and the effective poison.»360
Then he explains that «they named it the poison of the vipers» and that this
is for «the nine soakings».360 From all this we can see that the poison is the
divided up divine water, the nine flying eagles with the sharp swords that we
saw in Ibn Umail’s great vision. The union of the soul and the spirit is the
living insight into the meaning of a fiery emotion. When this water is
reunited step by step with the concrete reality of the individual—the body—
this turns into a deadly poison for all that is false in the psyche of the adept,
killing off all unconscious, shadowy elements. Whoever is able to find that
poison «has found the right way (tariq al-haqq)», taking responsibility for
one's individual path, in contrast to those who follow the collective law and
order (sanca). The poisonous or lethal aspect of the divine water was
.’59 Hall ar-rumuz, p. 57.4-6. 360 Ibid. p. 59.5.
361 Ibid., p, 67.4.

[Page 144]
The Dyer and the Dyed
143
Fig. 51: The nine soakings are for Ibn Umail the nine dyes or the poison of the

is represented by nine (3+6! ) scorpions, represented in the round mirror that he saw

[Page 145]
144 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumu:
already in Greek alchemy and later in Latin alchemy a central feature.362 It is
also well known in Chinese alchemy.363
Text: (19.6) And concerning what Halid ibn Yazld said: «The
philosophers who passed away in all periods of time failed to dye gold,
unless it was gold.» He was referring to the gold of the sages, which is their
magnesia. But the people imagined that it is gold which is in their hands.
Therefore they ruined it with different kinds of operations and they became
tired and did not succeed. And in another place they said: «Make the gold
white.» And they also said: «Turn the gold into silver (waraq)». By that
they meant the making white of this magnesia when the blackness
appeared on it during the operation. Thus these statements of the sages
prove to you the rightness of my sayings that the sages name their white
stone, when its whiteness becomes extreme, gold. As they say: «Dye the
gold into red.» And as they say while it is blackness: «Turn the gold into
silver.» That means that you must turn the stone white and remove its
blackness till it turns into the colour of silver, because they named their
magnesia during the state of blackness and the state of whiteness gold.
Under that blackness there are powers and dyes, about which only God the
Exalted knows. The sages were unable to know that. Therefore they said
that the more you soak their stone, the more dye you get from it. By that it
rises in rank and this increases forever without end. (21.12)
The text quotes Hälid ibn Yazïd (ca. 673-720), who was said to be
the son of the Umayyad Caliph Yazld. He was one of the first Arabs to take
up the study of alchemy.364 The gold of the sages is the acquired union of
spirit and soul, the creation of a new consciousness, which is then reunited
with the reality of the purified individual psyche of the adept, called the
second body. «Dye the gold white» and «dye the gold red» become under­
standable when we learn from Ibn Umail’s al-Mâ: al-waraqï that they not
only named their divine water but also their earth gold. This is at the time
when their earth was soaked with the nine parts of the water: «I told you
362 In a text attributed to Ostanes, entitled “On the Holy and Divine Art", the author states
that this divine water kills the bodies and revives the dead. M. Berthelot, Alch. Grecs IV.II,
and in Latin alchemy Paracelsus, for instance, called the lumen naturae (light of nature) lethal.
363 1 refer here e.g. to the wonderful story of Wei Po-Yang (2. Cent.) and his dog. (J. Needham
et al.. Science and Civilisation in China, Vol. 5.II, p. 295).
364 For details about this see F. Sezgin, Gesch. des Arab. Schriftions IV, p. 120-126, and
M. Ullmann, Natur- und Geheimwiss. im Islam, p. 192-195.

[Page 146]
The Dyer and the Dyed 145
that they named their earth gold when they turned it white. And when it
turned black [the second time] by [one of] the three parts of the water, they
said about it: “Turn the gold white.” Thus when it turned white, they said:
“Dye the gold.” Then they said: “Return the water to it, and it will increase
its dyeing.” Thus the water is their cultivated gold in the pure white earth,
which is the earth of the gold. And in it, and from it, its flowers and its dyes
appear with the help of the spiritual water.»365 The knowledge of Ibn
Umail's al-MâD al-waraqT, in which he explains his great vision, can again
be seen to be crucial to an understanding of his thoughts in our text here.
With regard to the second blackness, we also find an explanation in 175
his al-MäJ al-waraqT: «But indeed this [second] blackness appears—as we
explained to you at the beginning—from the new soft second body,
because of the darkness of the soul which is in it. And with regard to the
grossness of the body, they ordered that it be washed in order that it could
cling to the first operation, with which it appeared, until the body becomes
fine and subtle and the water burns its thickness and grossness. And the
unoperated soul, which is in it, becomes refined and improved by the
burning of the body by that spiritual water. Thus when both of them [soul
and body] become refined and subtle together, this unstable second black­
ness gets hidden and returns concealed inside the second body, about
which the sages told us that all of this blackness is a dye. And the white­
ness, which disappeared and was concealed, reappears, and this concealed
blackness is that one which is looked for. And it is the greatest king, yet the
greatest king is concealed and hidden in this blackness, and it is the gold,
which rendered it into the colour of silver when it turned white and it made
it silvery. And we need to return it to this gold, which is concealed in this
blackness, which they name the Ethiopian earth. And this gold which they
have is extracted from this blackness in their white earth, which is a part of
the moony Jupiter. And it is the great king, who needs to be given back his
crown, which is in the supreme, heavenly water, and which is the crown of
victory. And the king is the ashes which come from the ashes. And they
named him by the name of his sister, the crown of victory, and by the pure
eternal water, because of its being resistant and eternal in the fire and being
patient with it. Also they said “the pure”, because it was purified from the
darkness of the soul and from the darkness of its tomb, which is the thick
body from which it was extracted. And it grows up and becomes stronger
Al-Ma: al-waraqi,
p. 56.23-26.

[Page 147]
146 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
by sucking the milk that comes out, when it is returned to it and made to
enter—he and his sister—into the second new body. Then the colours and
the dyes appear in it. This blackness, in which the king is concealed, which
is the dyer-gold of the sages, is the darkness which none of the first sages
could grasp by his understanding or know what powers and dyes are under
that darkness. Therefore I taught you to know that every time they soak it,
the dye redoubles for ever and ever, without end. Thus praise be to God for
giving preference to this composed stone and bestowing this great secret on
it.»366 From this text we can see that the reappearance of the blackness, after
the whiteness of the first operation, is of crucial importance. It is a second
purification work with the aid of the newly-established insight (= the gold)
that was produced in the first work. The new attitude that is now appearing
as a new centre of the personality is established, so to speak, outside the
bodily existence. It is able—when brought back to the individual reality—
to connect to all the impurities that are still in the second body. That causes
the second blackness, a depressed state, where the result of the first part of
the work seems to be completely lost. But beneath this blackness there is
now the potential for all the colours and all of life. This second blackness is
not going to be eliminated by the further soakings. Our text says that it will
remain, and that when it is concealed inside, it will even become the new
centre, the king, «the moony Jupiter», meaning Jupiter connected with the
moon. We know from a manuscript of Abü Abdullah Muhammad al-
MarrakusT, that the goal of the work is the union of King Jupiter with Queen
Moon, Jupiter symbolizing the renewed sun that functions again properly as
the ruling principle by being united with the light of the night.367
With regard to the statement that «the more you soak their stone the
more dye you get from it, and by that it rises in rank and this increases
forever without end», we find a detailed explanation in Ibn Umail’s al-McP
al-waraqï: «... as Democrites said: “Our gold is like a plant sprouting in
the garden and the gold of the people is like a sprout of the mountains.”
Thus because their gold is like a plant sprouting in the garden, regarding
the watering and the moisture and the beauty, what it led to was similar to
it. Its branches, in respect to beauty and benefits, were like their origin,
which is their elixir. Thus if you have left over operated water or water
AI-M3} al-waraqï,
366 p.73.8-24.
367 Abü Abdullah M uham m ad al-M arrâkusï; M s 562 al-Biruni Institute, Taschkent. The
lunar Jupiter corresponds in Pharaonic times to renewal of the Pharaoh in the night-journey,
with its goal of reconnecting him with the Uraeus-snake, his female aspect or consort.

[Page 148]
The Dyer and the Dyed
147
Fig. 52: Tree with three boughs. «Thus it will multiply for you like that forever, in
the same way as a tree. The more you water it, the more it gives you its fruit, never
stopping as long as it exists. Thus its growing will be without end ... .» Quote from
Democritus in Ibn Umail’s al-MäJ al-waraqï, p. 61.20. Picture from Zosimos’

[Page 149]
148 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
extracted from another earth that you saved, and you soak this earth with a
part like one of those first parts, the intensity of dye will increase for you.
Thus it will multiply for you like that forever, in the same way as a tree.
The more you water it. the more it gives you its fruit, never stopping as
long as it exists, (see fig. 52) Thus its growing will be without end: The
more you water it, the more it will give you a dye, a multiplication of what
it is. And whenever you stop doing this, it also stops. And if you were to
increase it, it would increase for you forever without end. Therefore
Mltäwus, the head of the distillers, said to the King Marqünus, “Indeed one
Mithquäl of it fills what is between East and West.” And that came after
the king asked him: “Would its increasing be for ever?” Mltäwus
answered: “Yes.“»368 Here the alchemical work is compared to the growth
of a tree that continues to grow as long as the adept lives. This characterizes
the spiritual life of humans. Contrary to biological life that develops and
then decays, the spiritual life continues to grow if nourished. Continuous
soaking of the personality with the living, emotionally touching (= fiery)
water of the inner pictures from dreams and active imaginations enables the
tree of life to keep growing till the end of life. By this continuous reddening
with the help of the fiery water, the immanent inner myth of the individual
can develop like a tree «never stopping as long as it exists».
Text: (21.12) This is a great secret from the secrets of God the
Exalted. It is a characteristic that God put in this stone, which appears from
it by the operation. It has been hidden from all the people because they [the
sages] recommend to each other not to put it in a book. And they deferred
its matter [of the stone] to God the Exalted that He gives it to whom he
wishes and withholds it from whom he wishes. (23.1 )
Here we learn why the alchemists kept secret the stone and its
wonderful power: It has to be revealed by God. This withholding of the
secret of the stone is quite understandable when we consider that for Ibn
Umail’s respected Zosimos, the stone is directly called «the shadow of the
sun».369 The sun is a symbol for the highest value, the collective image of the
divine. The stone as the shadow of the sun symbolizes therefore all that is
excluded from the collectively accepted image of God, all that is generally
Al-MâP al-waraqï,
iss p. 61.15-25; W e also find this thought in earlier alchem ical text. One
Epistle of the Spheres
of the earliest sources is probably the by Hermes of Dendera, a text
al-McE al-waraqï. Studien,
quoted in See I. Vereno, p. 178.
Mushaf as-suwar,
369 fol. 73a.19.

[Page 150]
The Dyer and the Dyed 149
rejected and despised, that is why it is also called the devil's stone. It
contains those qualities of the divine that are not contained in the highest
value of the present collective consciouness, but that are all the same
devilishly tempting and attractive to the individual. Every image of the
divine ages with the passage of time, and fades into mere formalism, but the
stone that is worked upon and purified, i.e. understood in its symbolic value,
will complete and renew the ageing image of the Divine. That is why Ibn
Umail also calls the stone in our text «the sister of prophethood» as prophets
were always the ones to point to the missing values of the collective.370
Text: (23.2) The dye is their water and the dyed is their earth. And 179
by it [the water] appear its flowers and its dyes. Thus the water dyes the
earth and the earth likewise dyes the water with its colours and its sulphurs,
which are in it [the earth], and it [the earth] held the dye and did not let it
escape while the water disappears at the time of its drying. (23.6)
In his al-Mä7 al-waraqï, Ibn Umail explains the process of dyeing iso
that he alludes to in this passage: «By it [the water] the sage meant the soul
and the spirit which carries the soul, because they say that the spirit is a
boat for the soul. And it is the water which extracted this soul from its
bodies. And this soul is the dissolved dye which is carried in it, in the same
way as the dyes of the dyers are carried in their waters. And they dye their
garments with that water in which are their dyes. Thus that water spreads
and expands in the garment, and that dye runs with it, and they enter
together into the garment. The [...] water disappears by the drying up and
the dye remains in the garment. This is the same with the water of the
sages, which carries their dye. They return that water carrying the dye to
their thirsty, holy, silvery, white earth, which they compared to the garment.
Their water enters into their earth and spreads out all over it. And with it
