other symbols of the stone that we saw earlier. Tar is a name for the divine
water, and at the same time for the stone in its state of second blackness.
This is easier to understand if we remember Ibn Umail’s explanation that
when the divine water enters the second body for the first time, it creates
the second blackness.528 Pitch and tar as names for the divine water are also
found elsewhere in Greek and Arabic alchemy.529 It is characteristic of
alchemical thinking that not only the pure sparkling water but also the
devilish black pitch and tar are symbols for the divine water, as this water
unites the light and the dark, being a composition that contains both «the
fugitive and the seeker, the friend and the enemy, the male and the female,
the one who fecundates and the one fecundated, the changer and the
changed», as Zosimos states.530 This is frequently experienced in analysis.
The black, horrifying aspects of what emerges from the unconscious, or
what is experienced in daily life, can become precisely what has a trans­
forming effect on the patient.
After pitch and tar, Ibn Umail speaks of the flying eagle (an-nasr 298
at-tä^ir) and the falling eagle (an-nasr al-wäqic) as names for the divine
water. The flying eagle is a symbol for the soul and the spirit which was
distilled out of the first body. In Ibn Umail’s great vision we saw this
divine water —divided up into nine parts—as flying eagles, represented on
the ceiling of the antechamber of the Pharaonic sanctuary (see fig. 5). The
divine water is then returned to the second body as «the falling eagle» at
528 This connection of the second blackness with tar and the divine water most probably has
its roots in the Ancient Egyptian mummification ritual. When the mummy was soaked in
salting water, containing natron, it turned black. The word natron goes back to the Egyptian
word ntr,meaning god. Thus in the mummification ritual, the corpse was literally soaked in
god-water, then turning black like tar. See M.-L. von Franz, Dream and Death,p. 86.
529 We find e.g. in the Turba philosophorum,which goes back to Greek and Arabic sources,
an explanation why their liquid (= the divine water) is named pitch, balsam and gum (see
J. Ruska. Turba,p. 234). In the Mushaf as-suwarwe find tar mentioned in various places: e.g.
fol. 1 lb.7-10, fol. 58b.5-9, 92b.3, etc.
530 Zosimos, Mafâtïh as-sanca,at the end of the ninth treatise on fol. 78a.

[Page 225]
224 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumûz
the time of the second part of the work. The falling eagle symbolizes this
return to the white corpse, the second body.531
299 From all the other names, like the different oils and gums that point
to different aspects of the divine water, only the oleander (difla) and the
leaves of oleander (waraq ad-difla) need be amplified in detail. In the
Mushaf as-suwar of Zosimos we find these two symbols in different places.
Zosimos explains to Theosebeia: «“Know that the oleander leaves keep the
burning heat of the fire away from its companions. I know that from a book
of Äbulün (Apollo) in which he gave analogies, telling the secrets in them.’’
She said: “Who is Apollo?” He said: “He is the sun, and the sun is Aqastus,
and Aqastus is the fire. Thus Apollo is the seeker of the virgin (this shows
that this analogy refers to the story of Apollo and Daphne, waraq ad-diflâ
should thus be read as laurel leaves), but he could not catch her, only ole­
ander leaves.” She said: “Do you think that Apollo was passionately in love
with the virgin?” He said: “Yes, he was in accordance with her nature, but
he was afraid of the fieriness of her firewood.” She said: “What is that fire­
wood of hers?” He said: “It is the firewood of the oleander.” She said:
“Does he compare the fieriness of the virgin with the oleander firewood?”
He said: “Yes.” She said: “What is the passionate love of the virgin for
Apollo like?” He said: “She is more passionately in love with him than he
with her, because her nature is in accordance with him and also she is free,
noble, honest, and afraid of Apollo’s fire.”»532 From this text we can see that
the oleander leaves, which are able to fight the fire, grow right out of what
is carrying the fire, namely the oleander wood. Thus the oleander leaves are
a symbol for what enables consciousness (= Apollo = the sun) to fight or to
resist the fire. This is confirmed later in the same text by Zosimos, who
tells Theosebeia that the oleander leaves are the white sulphur that
makes the copper white.533 In the Koran, the oleander is connected to hell
(Sura 37:62-68, 44:43-46, 56:51-56) and the devil who is created out of fire,
531 The flying and the falling eagle became such important symbols in Arabic culture that
they ended up becoming the names of two bright stars in Arabic astronomy. Later on these
names entered the Western night sky: The flying eagle can be found as the brightest star, called
Atair (arab. at-täHr = the flying one), in a group of stars that is called the eagle, while the
falling eagle is found as the brightest star, called Wega (arab. al-wäqic = the falling one) in the
star group called the lyra. These two stars form part of the famous summer triangle.
532 Fol. 92b.5-14; the same is also mentioned on fol. 169a.l5-169b.2.
533 «Zosimos said: “[...] with regard to the oleander leaves, it is the white sulphur [...].
And Maria said in a thousand places: ‘Burn the copper with the sulphur.’ Know that our
heavenly sulphur does not bum with a corrupting burning but a beneficial, whitening, useful

[Page 226]
Earth and Heaven 225
but who is also the master of fire. This mystery that there is a possibility of
the union of drive (fire) and drive control (fighting the fire) is the centre
around which symbolic-religious alchemy revolves.
Text: (51.5) They named the parts of their water which operates on 300
their stone the physicians, because they [the parts of the water] cure it [the
stone], since they operate on it and take away disease and darkness which
are in it, and revive it after its death, and transform it from one thing to
another from one nature to another and from weakness to strength. They
named these parts of the water also the sages, and every part of it they
named a sage. (51.11)
By the different parts of the water, which are called here the 301
physicians (atibbä7) and the sages (hukamäD), Ibn Umail means the nine
soakings of the second part of the work. Seven waters of the waters he
connects to the seven planets and two waters to the dragon’s knots, as he
explains later (on p. 61.2). The extracted or sublimated fire or sulphur,
united with the water in order to become the divine water, is now reunited
in the second part of the work with the bodily reality, turning by that
process into the healing power. This indicates the healing effect of a
symbolic understanding of any drivenness. For this effect, these waters
are also named «the sages».
Text: (51.11) Likewise they named their stone the sages’ egg. They 302
meant that it is from these things [seven waters], which they named sages.
And they did not mean that it is the sages’ egg of the people. This is the
explanation for their saying the egg of the sages. It is like their naming the
male a cock and the female a hen. And they named it [the water] froth of
the bubbles, butter and coarse grease. Maria the sage named it, I mean their
water, calcified mercury, bat dung, fat of castor beans, fat of eggs, water of
eggs and named it every fat and bitterness, and the brain, the soul, the
blood, the dye, the air, the fire, the seven crowns for the seven parts of the
water, the water of the Nile, the rain, the reviving abundant rain, and the
water of a raincloud because they named their copper—I mean their
stone—a raincloud. (53.7)
burning.” She said: “And what is its benefit?” He said: “It turns it into gold, and it removes its
shadow. Thus Democritus burnt the copper with the sulphur, and with it he made it white, and
with it he removed its shadow, and with it he coloured it.”» Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 181a.l8-
181b.4. The oleander leaves making the copper white are also mentioned on fol. 125a. 10.

[Page 227]
226 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
303 For our author, the egg of the sages (baid al-hukamä:) is the result
of a process «as it is from these things (the seven waters), which thev
named the sages». It is the union of different parts of the divine water that
is achieved in the first part of the work. This is also symbolized, as we saw
earlier, in the successful marriage of the four elements.534 In Ibn Umail's
ad-Durra an-naqïya, we find an instructive passage on how he understood
the symbolism of the egg: «The people [of the science] said: “They called
the stone an egg because the egg unites three colours: Shell, egg-white, and
egg-yolk. The yellow is the stone of the sun, and the white is the stone of
the water, and the shell is the stone of the earth which surrounds it. But this
is only an analogy with the egg. The [concrete] egg has no place, rank or
relevance in the knowledge of the art. [...] Understand that!» Ibn Umail
explains that the shell is Adam, the egg-white is Eve, and the yolk is the
fiery sulphur. He continues: «Adam is Saitânïl to Eve (Ädam li-Hawwä:
Saitânïl). Thus Saitânïl is the stone which is called the solid fire, and the
hot and soft yolk, without which the chicks of the stone could not be
produced. [...] Know that the stone which is called the egg of the sages, is
Adam-Saitäml-Eve. Adam is the gold which is the body alluded to symboli­
cally as the shell of the egg. Saitânïlis the sulphur which is the yolk,535 and
its nature is the nature of the egg-yolk, hot and moist: Its fire indicates its
heat, and its dissolving into oil indicates its moisture. They called it the
middle olive of the fat, because it is between Adam and Eve, like the heart
is in the middle of the inside of a human being. Eve is the virgin mercury,
which is the cold and moist egg-white, because it looks most similar to the
colour of the egg-white. So understand the egg which does not come out of
a bird. [...] and you must guard this treasure, which does not leave you as
long as you live.»536 This passage shows that for Ibn Umail, the egg is a
symbol for the successful integration of the yolk, fiery drive-soul (nafs).
which has a direct relationship to evil (Saitän). The stone is thus a two-
oneness that is a symbol of the realization that consciousness and the
unconscious always belong together. The two are held together by the
eggshell, symbol for the reality of the unique unity of the adept. The egg.
as result of the first part of the work, then has to remain in the air, as we
534 See Risäla Qalübatra malikat Samannüd, M. Ullmann, “Kleopatra”, p. 173: Cleopatra
recommends caring for the equilibrium of the four basic qualities of heat and cold, wetness and
airiness in order that the creation of the egg (al-baid) becomes successful.
535 The yolk contains aggressive sulphur. Never put a silver spoon into a raw egg!
536 Ad-Durra an-naqïya, fol. 5 f.

[Page 228]
Earth and Heaven 227
read in Ibn Umail's al-Mä7 al-waraqî: «... and about it another sage said:
“This egg travels around the whole world.” By that he means the air,
which is between the sky and the earth. And it is in every place. [...] As
the other sage says: “I say: It is the egg born in the air from the spiritual
primordial matter of the earth.” About it—I meant about this new-born
which they named dye, blood and egg—Hâlid ibn Yazïd said: “Take the
blond egg from the best source that you find, neither in the sky nor in the
earth.” In saying “the blond egg”, Halid ibn Yazld meant the new-born,
which is delivered for them in the air. Thus it is neither on the earth nor
in the sky.»537 In another place in al-Mâ: al-waraqï Ibn Umail goes on to
explain why the egg is such an important symbol for the stone: «The egg
is like that: Without anything strange entering it, it transforms from one
state to another, and is changed from one thing to another. Then it turns
into a flying bird, like what was its origin and its beginning.»538 Out of
the egg in a second birth, the phoenix is born. This is a symbol for the
life that emerges from the stone. This resurrection of the second body
takes place for Ibn Umail in the second part of the work. The origin of
this connection of the stone of the sages to the egg and the phoenix can
be traced back to Ancient Egypt, as Helmut Jacobson was able to show.539
The symbolism of the egg was then well known in Greek alchemy, and
found its way from there into Arabic alchemy.540
537 Al-Mä0 al-waraqi, p, 38.2-10.
538 Ibid., p. 27.23-24
539 See H. Jacobsohn, "Das göttliche Wort und der göttliche Stein im alten Aegypten".
Eranos Jahrbuch 1970, p. 234. In addition to Jacobsohn’s paper, one has to mention the long
tradition of the connection of the yolk of the egg to the sun in Ancient Egypt. Pharaoh
Thutmosis III, who was the first ruler to bring back chickens to Egypt after his conquest of
Syria, named this new species «the bird that gives birth to a sun every day». The yolk of the
egg was considered to be a little sun.
540 E. O. von Lippman gave a summary of egg symbolism in Greek alchemy: «Our copper,
our lead, our magnesia is incubated by the warmth. That is why it is named “egg of the sages”,
“sages' egg” or “substance bom from a bird” (hylë ornithogonia). As the egg is a unified
whole, yet at the same time consists of shell, skin, white and yolk—that is a four-oneness that
is just one—in the same way, the sages’ egg is a tetrasomia of copper, lead, tin and iron, yet it
is just one. (to de tessera hen)[...] The sages’ egg is already gold and silver as it contains gold
and silver inside, however, only as a possibility (dynamis) that is concealed in its inside (endon
kekryptai).For that reason, nothing else is needed except to turn «the nature from inside out»
(pherein exo tën phy siri),in order that you really obtain what is sought.» (E. O. von Lippmann,
AlchemieI, p. 343 f.).

[Page 229]
228 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
304 Then Ibn Umail adds different names of the water, which are
connected to every fat and bitterness. The fat is a union of the fiery hot soul
united with the spirit to become the divine water. The bitterness points to
the bitter truth coming from the divine water, i.e. emerging from the
insights distilled out of symbols of the unconscious. Zosimos connects the
bitterness directly to the water of the sulphur and the fiery poison.541 Later,
among other things, «the brain» is mentioned as a name for the stone. In all
the books of Ibn Umail known to me at this point, this name cannot be
traced. But in a Greek text of Zosimos, named “On the Asbestos”, the reason
for this name is given: «The alabaster-stone is called the brain because it is
able to hold the fugitive dyes. [...] The spirit of the fire [that is for Ibn
Umail the soul] got united with the stone and became a unique spirit.»542
305 «The water of the Nile» as a symbol for the divine water is evident
to anybody living in Egypt. As Cleopatra explains to the sages: «[It is
named like that] because when the Nile floods the land it fecundates the
seeds. Then there is a rich crop and the land is blessed. Our water is like
that. When it enters our process, something good comes out of it.»543 This
describes the vivifying and beneficial influence of the images from the
unconscious upon the dry world of consciousness.
306 This also explains the next name as symbol for the divine water:
«the rain, the reviving abundant raincloud». It is mentioned by Ibn Umail
in his poem about his great vision, in his Risäla as-sams ilä al-hiläl:
His soul rises from him and climbs high
to heaven. So he is a sun that rises
to the full moon for a time.
And to her he has recourse
is humbled in passionate love for her and submits.
Then the two lights are paired in the lofty height.
Then the water comes to possess two lights
radiating just as you see the sun,
Raining down on the ashes of the dead
so what was after these two comes to life,
Like the dead under the watering of the sky.544
541 In his Mushaf as-suwar,fol. 60a.l0-15; on fol. 51al5 Zosimos speaks of the bitter stone.
542 M. Berthelot, Alch. grecs,III.n.1.
543 Risäla Qalübatra malikat Samannüd, M. Ullmann, “Kleopatra”, p. 169.
544 Al-Mä^ al-waraql p. 8.19-24.

[Page 230]
Earth and Heaven 229
In al-MäD al-waraql, Ibn Umail comments further on this motif: 307
«From those ashes the living, reviving rain is raised up. And it descends
from the sky after it has ascended to it, whereby it obtains the power of the
above and the below when it inhales the air. When it descends to earth it
revives it after it was dead, and all that is on it lives with it. And by all of
this they meant this stone.»545 From this we can see that the beneficial rain
is another symbol for the king who also ascended and then descended.
Each symbol points to a different aspect of the otherwise indescribable
stone and its action, which is the divine water flowing from it.
Text: (53.8) And when the bodies became mercury, they named 308
them sulphur because the sulphur [fem.] held the sulphur [masc.]. It is the
holding of the water by the water. And the two are the spirit and the soul
when they blended and became ripe by rising up. When the water is purified
from its filth and becomes white, they name it white lead. And when they
operate on it [the water] until it becomes pure, they name it pure water,
moist vapour and dry vapour. By the moist one they meant the spirit in
which they have made the spirit of the stone rise, which is its soul. And by
the dry one they meant the soul, because the soul is the moisture of the dry
bodies. They related it to its origin, the dry body. And they named the
water with which they caused [the soul] to rise, with it and in it, and with
which they dissolved it from their body, a moist vapour, relating it to its
origin which is the water. They also named the soul the smoke and the
sediment relating it to its dry origin because the smoke comes out of dry
firewood. And they named the spirit in which they caused it [the soul] to
rise the moist vapour, relating it [the spirit] to its origin, which is the water.
And the moist vapour is from the water and the soul is the smoke, because
it came out of the dry body. And therefore they said: «The vapour and the
smoke». And they named both of them the two vapours. (55.11)
This part of our text is understandable in the light of the great vision 309
of Ibn Umail, where we saw that the two vapours uniting in the air are the
sublimated fire (= smoke) and water (= vapour). Brought together in the
air, they unite as two vapours, and the smoke is at that time also named
vapour. By the water that is held by the water Ibn Umail means the same
as when he points out that the sages named «both of them (the smoke and
545 Al-MäJ al-waraql p. 46.26-28; the symbolism of the rain could could go back to the
Risäla al-Faklyät, edited by I. Vereno, Studien, p. 160. In this text, Uwîrs (Osiris), the master
of the water, is a personification of the water (p. 313).

[Page 231]
230 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumüz
the vapour) the two vapours». Here he names the two waters also the two
sulphurs, meaning by these names the soul and the spirit. The water
holding the water is a symbol for the blending of soul and spirit. This
double or unified water is the divine water that in this part of our text refers
to the first part of the work, the separation of soul and spirit from the first
body, which is named the dry firewood here (see fig. 77).
3
\ /
\ /
\ /
1st body
1
Fig. 77: The schema of Ibn Umail's great vision, reduced to the ascending of the
two vapours, the soul and the spirit (the whole schema is given in on page 54).
310 The «bodies turning into mercury» (as we read at the beginning of
our passage) is a result of the first part of the work. It points to a stage when
the different aspects of the individual personality are loosened up, and
therefore become liquid like quicksilver. Then their passive dynamism, «the

[Page 232]
Earth and Heaven 231
female sulphur» (a symbol for the spirit or the drive-regulation) becomes
conscious or different from the active-fiery drives, «the male sulphur» (the
drive-soul or nafs). Drive and drive-regulation can then be reunited when an
idea of the meaning behind an autonomous drivenness becomes visible. This
goal of a first union was symbolized in Ibn Umail’s great vision of the sages'
stone tablet by the two birds holding each other by the beak.
Text: (55.12) And likewise they named their stone the sea of the two 311
vapours. And they named this soul the red sulphur, the arsenic, the sandarach,
and every sulphur that rises up in the vapour of the people, and other salts
and the ammonias. Thus this is their saying: «Raise up the sulphur, the
arsenic, the ammonia, and the mercury in the vapour.» The vapours are in
reality the spiritual water of the sages, by which they caused the body to
rot. And they destroyed it and by that they extracted its soul and took it
out in one place. This is what Ares said to the king about this soul: «If you
do not extract it from its sediments you will not have mercury from
cinnabar». This spirit, which is the dyeing water for the soul, is [also]
dyed by it. At that time they become one dye. And they named it fire,
dyeing poison, and fiery poison, and it is the flying eagle, the benefit with
many names [...] (57.7)
In this passage, the stone is first named «the sea of the two vapours». 312
The sea is a symbol of the body that is the mother of the two vapours, the
soul and the spirit. The work of the adept consists in elevating the soul that
is symbolized by the different sulphurs and the other names that we saw
before. Ammonia, however, is a new name for the soul. This is a very
volatile, aggressive substance with a strong, unpleasant smell. It is a symbol
for the devilish-demonic drivenness that has to be separated, extracted and
raised up from the body in order to be connected to the regulating images
that belong to it—symbolised here by the mutual dyeing of soul and spirit.
Then this union of spirit and soul (the divine water) is named «fire, 313
dyeing poison and fiery poison».546 All these names point to a quality of
the divine water, which has—as we have seen in different places—that
devilish poison and fire of the soul inside. It is a description of the effect
546 The fact that this poison has to be produced intentionally is already well known in Greek
alchemy. Zosimos points this out in his Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 215a.l7-215b.5 «Zosimos said:
“Thus take that saffron and put it in a vessel and soak it with the eternal water. Know that if
you soak it and roast it this will be good for it. Thus cook it till its colour satisfies you, and be
careful not to soak it until it dries up. And whenever it dries up, you soak it till all your water

[Page 233]
232 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
derived from the insights gained into the symbolic meaning of an inner
demonic fire: It kills the futilities of the first body (that is the old ego).
Because of that it is named by Ibn Umail «a benefit with many names».
Text: (57.7) [...] and [they named it] the fiery water, and the fiery
is the ashes of the ashes, which is its soul and its spirit that are extracted
from it [the body]. And it is the moon. Therefore they named their water
spittle of the moon, froth of the river, froth of every moisture and dryness,
foam of the sea, dung of every animal, dung of a dog, moisture of a gall
bladder of every beast, and purer than the fire, the magnet and the cloud.
They explained it with everything they were able to. (57.13)
The divine water is also called «the fiery water» here, because it is
a union of water and fire, spirit and soul. Then it is named «the ashes of the
ashes, which is its soul and its spirit that are extracted from it [the body]».
«The ashes» are the purified first body that has been reduced to ashes and
made white by the process of the first work,547 while «the ashes of the
ashes» are the spirit and the soul, extracted from the ashes.548 The symbol
«ashes of the ashes» is also mentioned by Ibn Umail in his al-M& al-waraqT
in different places, and this gives us further insight into what our author is
indicating with this symbol. One of his statements reads: «It is a stone with
another stone inside it, and this sediment—from which emerges that which
rose up—remains non-moving. Then, after the soul, its brother appears
from it—that is the ashes of the ashes—which Maria named the rennet,
which coagulates their water in their earth.»549 From this passage we see
is used up. Then leave it in its place for 40 days, and it will become [...] the perfect poison.”
She asked: “And what is this poison?” He said: "It is the spiritual dye which cannot be touched,
submerging in the body, which, when it [the dye] is thrown on it [the body], is mixed with it
like the mixing of the water with the water.”» The poison is here the divine influx that pene­
trates the psyche of the adept.
547 This is beautifully described by Zosimos: «Know that when the composition becomes
burnt ashes without a soul, that this is the time to use the waters (pi.) and the dye. Hermes said
about this: “When the ashes turn into ashes, know that you have mixed the ashes well as it has
a great power in it. It is the same with the firewood: When it is burnt and turns to ashes, the
fire cannot bum those ashes anymore. The bodies of the composition are like that: When they
are burnt and turn into ashes, the fire is no longer able to bum them, and you have performed
an excellent work.» Mafâtîh as-sanca, fol. 81 a.21-8lb.O.
548 Later on in our text, Ibn Umail speaks of «ashes extracted from the ashes» (p. 65.1-2) as
name of their stone, or of «the water of ashes» (p.71.6) as the name of their divine water.
549 Al-Mä} al-waraqT, p. 47.24-26.

[Page 234]
Earth and Heaven 233
that what Ibn Umail must have meant by «the ashes of the ashes» is some­
thing spiritual that belongs to the second body, which is able to coagulate
the divine water when it returns to the earth. And at another point he
writes: «Hermes said: “Cultivate gold in a white silvery earth.” The white
silvery earth—Ibn Umail now observes—is the crown of victory, this
being the ashes which are extracted from the ashes and their second body.
They also name this their body, in which they cultivate this gold of theirs,
gold.»550 In this statement, the ashes of the ashes are the sediment, i.e. the
first body from which soul and spirit have been extracted and which has
been reduced to nothingness. It is the dead body that turns into the rennet
for the coagulation of the divine water that takes place in the second body.
The different statements of Ibn Umail about the ashes of the ashes can be
best explained in the following sentences: «And this is the meaning of the
statement of the sage: “The relative encounters its relative.” And it is the
meaning of their statement: “Nature rejoices in nature, nature clings to
nature, nature holds nature, and nature wins over nature.” And it is the
same meaning as their statement: “Being holds being, and being clings to
being.” By all of this they meant the nature which is in the water, and it is
the soul. And the nature which is in the body is the soul that is in it. And
the ashes of the ashes, which are the crown of the victory, rejoice in meeting
their sister, who is the supreme soul and spirit. And the nature which is the
lower body rejoices in the heavenly nature, because it is similar to it and
is from it; so it holds it and clings to it and wins over it. Its winning over
it is in its controlling it, and keeping it back with it in its subtle body,
which is operated on and refined until it becomes subtle-spiritual like it
[the heavenly nature]. Then the colours appear in the body, coming from
the part of the soul which is in the body. The earth here dyes the water
with the soul which is in it, and the water dyes the earth with the soul
which is in it. Thus each one of them dyed the other.»551 The ashes of the
first body are the essence of the individual nature of the adept that remains
after the purification work. This passage makes it clear that the ashes of
the ashes are a symbol for the purified “lower soul and spirit” of the adept
(in Jungian terminology: the purified ego of a man and his anima, or the
purified ego of a woman and her animus) that are here ready for the final
unio mystica with the divine, heavenly nature.
550 Al-Ma: al-waraqT, p. 25.16-18.
551 Ibid., p. 60.19-27.

[Page 235]
234 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
Then he calls the first body the moon, which shows that Ibn Umail
316
understands the origin of the divine water as coming from the opposite of
consciousness (the sun). The divine water is therefore named «spittle of the
moon», the liquid that comes from the moon, symbol of the unconscious.552
The next names of the divine water, which connect to froth, foam and
dung, point to something which is emerging or forthcoming. Again we see
that the sages’ water is not only named with the pleasant aspects of creation
but also with the unpleasant, rejected ones. From this it becomes clear that
this water can only be described by paradoxes as it has «all the benefits and
is purer than fire» yet it is also the most despicable «dung or gall bladder
of every beast». The goal of the work of the alchemist is to create a vessel, a
psychic attitude that is capable of holding the opposites together, and to
accept the impossible truth that both the purest water and the hellish,
stinking, bitter water are an emanation of Allah.553
317 Text: (57.13) [And they named it] the gum of acacia, the murderess
and torturer of her husband. And they named it Ostanes, and their earth
Maria. And they named it Zosimos and their earth Theosebeia, and [they
named it] Alexander and their earth Tadäb, and [they named it] Harltis and
their earth Biyä. [And they named it] the oriental youth and their earth the
western slave girl and the laughing slave girl. [And they named it] the
mercury of the East, and their earth the mercury of the West. [And they
named it] the sharp sword that is the killing poison and the effective poison.
And [they named it] the ploughing youth and their earth Hänä, and it is the
black androdamas whose explanation is the multi-coloured. And it is the
etesian stone that also means the multi-coloured, and the peacock because
of its multitude of colours. And the androdamas is the pure water of the
sulphur and it is the gum. (59.11)
318 «The gum of acacia (asqünïya) that is murdering and torturing her
husband» is another name for the divine water. This gum is then named
Ostanes, Zosimos etc. while their earth is named Maria, Theosobeia etc.
This gum of acacia is only mentioned once in our text and Ibn Umail
mentions this gum only in his Kitäb al-magnisiyä, but with no further
comment on its meaning. The gum of acacia, as can be seen by the end of
our text here, is another symbol for the pure water of the sulphur, the divine
For its Greek origin see e.g. Zosimos’ Mafâtïh as-sanca, fol. 42a,14 49b.18 f., 68a21.
552 f.,
This is also confirmed by a statement of Zosimos in his Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 160a.3-6.
553

[Page 236]
Earth and Heaven 235
water. Here it is then directly related to different couples that are mentioned
right after «gum of acacia». It therefore seems important to look for further
amplification of this aspect of the divine water.
We find this gum of acacia in the Mushaf as-suwar and in the 319
Mafatlh as-sanca of Zosimos in different contexts. In the Mushaf as-suwar,
Zosimos explains to Theosebeia: «“And the best of its names is the fiery
one because it is the torturer of her husband.” She said: “And what is its
name?” He said: “It is the gum of the acacia thorns and it is the water of
the silver and it is the water of sulphur. And that is because it dissolves the
body and turns it into water. Thus the best of its names is the fiery one
because it is the torturer of her husband.”»554 This shows that by the gum
of acacia Zosimos must have meant a sticky liquid with a fiery quality,
what is known as gummi arabicum (fig. 78). From a further passage in the
Fig. 78: The gummi arabicum is the acacia gum that is called the murderess of her
husband.
same book of Zosimos we learn that there is a direct relationship between
the gum of acacia and the sun. In this passage, Theosebeia asks Zosimos.
«“Tell me about when the sage says that if you turn over the natures you
extract the best part of them in a pure form.” He said: “By ‘turning over’
he meant that if you destroy the exalted nature, and turn the shadow concealed
in the body into a spirit, you will obtain the exalted dye from the nature. So
bum and torture the natures with the fire well. If you turn the bodies into
Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 18b.6-9.
554

[Page 237]
236 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
non-bodies and these non-bodies into bodies, you will achieve what you
are hoping for. So mix the fugitive with the one which stays till they
become one. If you do that, you will successfully perform the work. At that
point the dyes stay and the spirits become fixed, because it is the spirit that
makes it strong enough to resist everything.” She said: “Which one of them
is the spirit?” He said: “It is the stone which contains the spirit and it is the
gum which is from the acacia. If you kill the torturer of her husband then
you will have solid mercury and a strong magnesian body which does not
flee from the fire after that.” She said: “Perhaps this is what Hermes called
a benefit with many names?” He said: “Yes, and be careful not to be deluded
by the manifold things you find in the books, for that is worthless. Know
that the things are one, and that single thing is what dissolves everything,
what turns it white, what turns it red, and what completes it in addition to
God, the Exalted and Magnified.” She said: “And which one of them is
this?” He said: “It is the stone which decreases in the casting, and it is the
one which destroys and ruins every body except what comes from the sun.”
She said: “I see that this acacia gum corresponds to the sun.” He said: "If
it were not for a harmony between the two it would neither turn the sun into
silver, nor remove the shadow of the copper at the beginning of the operation.
So cook well, for by the fire, the gum becomes strong and turns into mixed
ashes, after having been a white stone.”»555 From this passage we learn that
the gum of acacia is directly related to the principle of consciousness, and
that it is able to transform the sun (= gold) into silver (the metal of the
moon). With the help of this arcane substance, the whole process of removing
the shadow of the copper takes place. A further passage in the same book
reveals that acacia is—in the same way as the earth—the basis of the work:
But it initially captures, before going on to set free, as Zosimos explained:
«Whoever attains this science and is then captured will not wait long till he
frees himself. Then he will, God willing, build mosques and cities, because
he will multiply gold in the same way as a man who cultivates a field of
wheat, and reaps a great harvest. Like that the gold has to be cultivated in
the white earth which is called acacia. Without this earth, which is the gum
nothing can be, because with the gum he increases the gold, and with it he
obtains the abundant sustenance which the exalted God bestows on us by
his inspiration.»556 The creation of gold is obviously related to the acacia,
which is another name for the moon, or for the black earth, that later in our
555 Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 18b.9-19a.10.
556 Ibid., fol. 79a. 12-18.

[Page 238]
Earth and Heaven 237
passage is directly named the soror mystica of the alchemist.557 * In modern
terms she corresponds to the anima of a man that first kills and absorbs the
weak, old and fragmented consciousness in the first coniunctio (fig. 79)
only to give birth to the new, single sun in the morning. In other words, the
earth kills the seed of wheat in order to bear abundant wheat afterwards.
«And with it he obtains the abundant sustenance which the exalted God
bestows on us by his inspiration», as the answer of Zosimos ended. After
this amplification of the gum of acacia we can sum up the psychological
meaning of this murderess of her husband. This sticky, spicy, destructive
Fig. 79: The fiery male dragon embracing the murderess of her husband that is the
gum of acacia, as she is called by Ibn Umail, and earlier by Zosimos.
(from Michael Maier’s Atalanta fugiens, 1618, Emblem 50)
557 The murderous activity of the asqiimya-gum is also described by Zosimos as being like
sulphur. Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 156a. 1 andl67b.5.

[Page 239]
238 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
Fig. 80: The sowing of gold into the silvery earth. The acacia earth is what first
kills her husband (the gold-grain) but then glues the dissolved husband together
again, just as Isis, the black earth, brings the parts of Osiris together for his resurrec­
tion as Horns, the new sun. (Atalanta fugiens 1618)
divine water is what attracts like gum or a magnet and then kills the old
consciousness, but also what will revive the old consciousness and bring
about new life.
320 We can now go back to our text, which continues with a key sentence:
«And they named it Ostanes, and their earth Maria. And they named it
Zosimos and their earth Theosebeia, and [they named it] Alexander and
their earth Tâdâb, and [they named it] Hands and their earth Biyä.» All
the names are different partnerships of alchemists, two of them being
known, the one Ostanes and Maria, and the other Zosimos and Theosebeia
(fig. 81). The name of Tâdâb being the earth of Alexander, as well as the
pair Hands and Biyâ cannot be traced in the list of names of male and
female alchemists given in the Fihrist.559 In al-MäJ al-waraqT, however, we
See J. Ruska, “Ein dem Chälid ibn Yazid zugeschriebenes Verzeichnis der Propheten,
559
Philosophen und Frauen, die sich mit Alchemie befassten“. Der Islam, Bd. XVIII, Heft 1/2,
1929, p. 293 f.

[Page 240]
raV‘■'
Earth and Heaven
239
J J ' c • . '■ •
Gy*; J jP Y. ''
Fig. 81 : Theosebeia and the corpse of Zosimos. This image shows how the archetypes
of sun and moon have entered the realm of two human beings. First this encounter
leads to the symbolic death of Zosimos, the death of the old Sungod. But then the
alchemical work leads to his resurrection. Theosebeia is like the mourning Mary,
holding her dead Zosimos in her arms. (From Zosimos’ Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 171b. )

[Page 241]
240 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
find the symbolic partner of Alexander, named Thathia, who according to
Stapleton, could be Thabia = Thebes, which becomes evident from one of
the symbolic description of the alchemical work.560
All these alchemical partnerships are to be understood as symbols
with relation to «the gum of acacia, the murderess and torturer of her
husband». The gum of acacia is the female, while Ostanes, Zosimos, etc.,
are the males. Our passage here corroborates what Zosimos tried to explain
to his beloved student Theosebeia in the Mushaf as-suwar,561 namely that
the archetypal mystery of torture and killing, as was, for instance, known
in the Isis and Osiris myth, has entered the realm of two human beings. In
Fig. 82: Zosimos and Theosebeia with two vipers. The relationship has three
levels: Sun and moon on their heads, two human beings, and the animal-level with
the bull and the two serpents. Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 70b.
560 «... The King asked Aras: “Tell me about Alexander and about Thathia.” Aras replied:
“You asked me about an enigma, so understand it! When Alexander reached Thathia, they
closed the gates and fought him. He then told them. ‘You have done as women do, because
if you were men you would have come out and fought us.’ [...]”» From al-Mä: al-waraqT,
p. 89.2-4. Then follows a description of the opus in the form of an allegory, the main symbols
being Alexander and Thathia, «which is a city that closed its gates as women do». A little later,
in the same text, different couples are mentioned, among them Aras, who married Bîyâ. These
could be the real names of our other pair Haritis and Biyä.
561 Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 70b.

[Page 242]
Earth and Heaven 241
the Mushaf as-suwar, we find different pictures illustrating this alchemical
relationship and their connection to the archetypal level, to sun and moon,
but also to the snake-level, the bodily aspects (fig.82).562 We are reminded
here of the statement of Maria mentioned previously (§ 14), where Ostanes
asks her: «What is the earth and what is the sky?» She replies: «For you I
am the earth and you are for me the sky.» In this alchemical partnership,
the woman is the earth for the man; she is the one who brings down to earth
his creative inspirations, she relates him to matter, materializing his logos,
adding the eros side to his life. The woman, on the other hand, receives and
carries out—in the same way as on the biological level—the creative
sparks of the man. This allows her to experience the fact that there is more
to life than mere biological existence; the man awakens the logos side in
her life.
Man and woman are the greatest living opposites that evolution has 322
produced so far. That is the reason we so often have couples mentioned in
our texts; uniting these opposites is probably the most frequent symbol of
the alchemical work. It seems that only this marriage is ultimately able to
express the mysterium coniunctionis of the divine and the human, their unio
mystica The meaning of this union of the opposites is, as C. G. Jung was
able to show, an inner marriage. Yet the desire or craving for this union
manifests itself first in a projected form on «some body or some thing», as
Ibn Umail put it, i.e. in the craving for a concrete possession of this «some
body or some thing». “The other” in a human being manifests itself first like
a magic magnet in the outer world. It comes in through the backdoor, so to
speak, possessing men and women. The fact that it happens so frequently
today—as we can see in the increasing divorce rate or the fascination for all
sorts of sex websites—indicates that the archetype of the acacia gum is
strongly constellated in our time. Any great fascination points to the presence
of this archetype of attractivity behind it. However, that fascination needs to
be reflected and understood symbolically in order that this archetype of the
feminine can be integrated into the collective consciousness.
Our next symbols, the mercury of the East (az-ZPbaq as-sarqi) and 323
the mercury of the West (az-ZPbaq al-garbï) are explained by Ibn Umail
in his commentary on al-Qasïda an-nünïya. There he reports the following
story: «Ostanes said: “I came to Egypt and I found the Egyptian Sheikh. I
562 From The Book of Pictures (Mushaf as-suwar) by Zosimos of Panopolis, CALA II. 1, fol.
87a and 86b on p. 78 and 79.

[Page 243]
242 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
spoke to him in my Persian language, but he did not understand what I said
to him. He spoke to me in his Egyptian language, but I did not understand
what he said to me. Then he took the stick from my hand and drew a line
on the earth, so I understood what he told me, and he understood what I
told him.” So somebody asked the sage: “What is the Egyptian Sheikh,
what is the Persian, and what is the stick?” He [Ostanes] said: “The Persian
is a man who came from the East, and it is the soul, and the Egyptian
Sheikh is the cold, white body. Thus the hot one escaped from the cold one.
But when I brought the stick, which is the mediating spirit, the hot one
mixed with the cold one, and the moist one with the dry one [united], and
they became one thing, which is the three: the spirit, the soul, and the
body.” So this also demonstrates the truth of what I explained to you
before, namely that the eastern mercury is the soul—T mean the soul of
their body—and the western mercury is the spirit, with which they extracted
the soul from the body, and it is his statement: “The fire is in an honoured
mountain, which we acquired with the water.” He means the sun of the
sages which is the soul, and the East is the origin of the sun. They also
related their body to the sun because it is hot and dry, while they related
the spirit to the moon because it is cold and moist, and it is the West.
Therefore they related the spirit to the West. By the West they meant the
moon, because it is the one that is moist. And this is the soul that is [in] the
stone of the sages, which Gäbir ibn Haiyän the Sufi named the eastern
mercury in his book. With the spirit, they extract this soul from its body,
and that is the operator of the body, and this spirit is the western mercury.
And those two, the soul and the ashes coming out from the ashes, are both
the brothers of Gäbir ibn Haiyän, which he mentioned in his books, and both
will appear at the end of time. By that he means their (= spirit and soul)
coming out of the body by the distillation, and the appearance of the ashes
from the ashes at the end of the first operation.»563 From this it becomes clear
that the mercury of the East is the fiery sulphur that has to be united with the
mercury of the West or the white sulphur that is the water of the moon.563
324 With regard to the symbolism of the two mercuries, we have a basis
for understanding in the two books of Gäbir ibn Haiyän with the titles
Kitäb az-ZPbaq as-sarqï (Book of the Mercury of the East) and the Kitab az-
ZPbaq al-garbï (Book of the Mercury of the West).564 Both books deal
563 Al-Qasîda an-numya, fol. 134a.6-134b.12.
564 M. Berthelot, La Chimie au moyen âge III, p. 207.

[Page 244]
Earth and Heaven 243
extensively with the soul and spirit, the soul-fire being the mercury of the
East, and the spirit-water being the mercury of the West. In the same way
as we have two sulphurs, one male and one female, we also have here two
mercuries, one related to the male fire, the other to the female water. And
the two mercuries have to be united in the work. All these bipolar symbols
show that it is an inner polarity which has to become conscious and whose
poles need to be reconciled by the opus. Ibn Umail’s symbolism of the
union of soul and spirit also seems to be related to the basic concepts of
Gäbir. The question of whether or not there is a connection between the
school of Gäbir and Ibn Umail via Abfl cAbdullah Muhammad al-
Marrâkusï will need further investigation.
In our text Ibn Umail then equates the androdamas (a Greek word 325
meaning the one who conquers the andros = the man) with «the pure water
of the sulphur and the gum» that overcomes the male. We find this symbol
also in the Mushaf as-suwar and the MafatTh as-san ca of Zosimos.566 In the
Mushaf as-suwar, he states: «This statement is what you asked me about,
because, if what has no body mixes with the smoke that quenches the thirst
of the thirsty one, it solidifies in it and becomes a body. There is a holder
between the two preventing them from escaping. This is the great gift the
sages named the stone of gold, androdamas, the stone of copper, claudianus,
pyrite, a dissolving stone, glue of the gold, and the stone of silver which
solidified after having been dissolved.»567 The black androdamas, on the
other hand, is a name for the divine water that is also known in Greek
alchemical texts. There it is, as in our text, connected to the multi-coloured
etesian stone. In the Lexicon of the Collection des alchemists grecs it is
equated with pyrite and arsenic, which is the active, sulphuric sub­
stance.568 The liquid and the solid form of the self are interchangable as the
whole process of alchemy is the symbolic description of a transformation
within the adept, where the self appears sometimes as dynamism and
sometimes as structure, in material form.
Text: (61.1) The ten names are the nine parts of the water that is 326
divided into nine cookings and each cooking is for a planet, seven for the
In the Mushaf as-suwar,e.g. on fol. 158a.7-10, where he equates androdamas with the
566
murderess of her husband; then also on fol. 160b. 13. In the Mafôiïh as-sancathe symbol is
found on fol. 53b, 58b, 59b, 61a. See also the Kitäb al-Habïband the Kitäb Qirätis al-hakïm.
Mushaf as-suwar,fol. 174a. 16-174b.2.
567
Alch. GrecsI.II.
568

[Page 245]
244 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
seven planets and two parts for the head and for the tail. And some of the
envious ones named them the nine days and the nine months and nine
letters. They [also] named them operations, just as someone of them said
about the purpleness and the finishing of the work: «And that comes after the
entering of the ninth month.» By that he meant its completion after the ninth
soaking. The ninth month is the number of days for the first work till the end
of the distillation. This is another aspect for them in this meaning. (61.9)
327 The nine soakings of the second body take place in the second part
of the work, as we have seen repeatedly. These nine waters correspond to
the seven planets and the so-called two knots of the dragon, its head and its
tail. The work in relation to the seven planets is commented on in Ibn
Umail’s Kitäb al-Mabäqil as-sabca (The Book of the Seven Planting
Lots). This book relates the nine parts of the soakings to the totality of the
mundus archetypicus, which means a gradual coming together of the reality
of the different archetypes (the planets) with the reality of the individual.
It is a union of the One and the Many.
328 Then comes a warning to beware of the envious ones (hasada).
These people can be understood as those who deliberately confused their
readers by creating new names and distortions. The alchemists hid their
secret for different reasons. One reason was to keep away those false
adepts who just wanted to use the connection to the archetypal world for
personal aims without serving God or the self. The danger of such an
attitude is explained in this book in § 79. Another reason for veiling their
stone is its nature, described as the lowest of the low and the highest of the
high (see § 221). Therefore the veiling of this individual paradox was
intended to protect the holiest of the holy from scornful judgements of the
ignorant. Besides these reasons, there were certainly also those alchemists
who started the alchemical work but somehow lost their way, and then
turned into fakes. So they just pretended to know the stone, and, turning
bitter, they wrote about alchemy in ways as nebulous as their minds,
consciously or unconsciously leading others astray, just as they themselves
had gone astray.569
329 Text: (61.9) The tenth, which is the completion of the ten. is the
first body, about which they said: «The one conquers the ten. and the ten
569 For the envious ones in Greek alchemy see J. Ruska, Turba Philosophorum, p. 290 f.
Ruska's conclusions about the Turba being “eine Kampfschrift" is, in the light of Ibn Umail's
text, given here in § 63 f., no longer valid.

[Page 246]
Earth and Heaven 245
conquer the one.» And the meaning of their conquering it is that they [the
ten] dissolve it, and its [the one's] conquering them [the ten] means that it
coagulated them and dries them up. (61.13)
«The tenth which is the completion of the ten» is, according to our 330
text, «the first body». The tenth one is that one which completes the nine
to the ten (see fig. 83). As we have seen in the great vision of Ibn Umail,
these nine are the elements water (2), air (3) and fire (4) which get completed
by the black earth, the missing one, that is the fourth (2+ 3+ 4 = 9 + 1
= 10). But by the entering of this tenth, the black earth, «they (the ten)
dissolve it (the first body)». The confrontation of the ego with the excluded
black earth dissolves this first body. This corresponds psychologically to
the destruction of the old ego-structure by the “devilish, irritating other
one”, which was not participating in conscious life and is now experienced
as being stronger. This can be, as we have seen in different examples,
Second body
Fig. 83: From the first to the second body by the way of the four elements, being
a symbol for the complete orientation of consciousness with the help of its four
functions, including the hitherto missing fourth element, the earth.

[Page 247]
246 Commentary on the Hall ar-rnmuz
anything that exerts an overpowering attraction on the ego. Therefore this
fascinating and at the same time feared “other one” led an autonomous life
in the psyche of the adept. This dark force of the “other one” is the one that
is able to overpower and dissolve the ten, symbol for the four elements that
are, seen psychologically, a symbol for consciousness based on its four
functions, sensation, feeling, thinking and intuition.770 This means the
excluded one (the black earth!) can dissolve the solid structure of the first
body. This leads to the first conjunction of the sun (the old consciousness)
with the moon, i.e. the “other one”, or the unconscious with all its blackness.
This bewildering state is often a reason for seeking professional advice.
Fig. 84: The ten conquer the
one, represented in later Latin
alchemy as the old king being
overcome by the ten clubs,
symbol for the completeness of
the four elements. This leads to
the first conjunction of sun and
moon.
«And its (the one’s) conquering them (the ten) means that it coagu­
lated them» is, as Ibn Umail explains, the later coagulation of the four
elements (1 + 2 + 3 + 4= 10) into a new unity in the second body. This is
the birth of the reality of the new centre, which emerges from the four
elements. Out of the four functions of consciousness that have become
conscious by the process, a new centre or god-image is bom that includes
that rejected dark aspect that led to the dissolution. The missing one that
was the tenth to create the first body becomes incarnate in the material
world of the individual. The nine and the one enigmatic unique reality of the
adept, the black earth that was at first unconscious and projected on «some­
body or something», make up the ten. Then in the second part of the work,
the ten coagulate in the one, meaning that the adept becomes conscious of
the unity of his personality that is at the same time the unity of the light and
the dark side of one life: «Nature conquers nature», as we saw earlier. 570
570 For a summary of the four functions of consciousness. I refer to the glossary.

[Page 248]
247
9. Fire and Water
Text: (61.14) And they named their water saffron of iron, sperm, 332
verdigris, white lead [ceruse], water of lead, Egyptian alum, and the water
of the vitriol. And Hermes the sage named it the soap of wisdom. He meant
by that the soul and the water with which the soul is dyed and the black­
ness gets washed. The water is the spirit in which there is the soul. And
therefore the sages said: «The water and the fire are sufficient for your
work.» By the fire they meant the soul and by the water they meant the
spirit in which there is the soul. [And by the saying] «The two are sufficient
for your work», he meant that both of them make the blackness disappear
and both make the whiteness appear. Thus these two are the water and the
fire, which the sages meant. (63.8)
The divine water is then named «saffron of iron, sperm, verdigris, 333
white lead [ceruse], and the water of lead». All these symbols point to some
specific quality of the divine water that is extracted from the first body. It
is named here saffron of iron, which means the red-yellow rust ‘growing'
out of iron.571 In later Latin alchemy, rust was called crocus martis. «The
sperm» as a symbol for the divine water points more to the living, creative
aspect of the extracted water. This symbol connects iron and copper, the
metals of Mars and Venus, because the next symbol for the divine water is
«the verdigris», which looks like a green substance ‘growing’ out of copper.
In Latin alchemy, it is called crocus veneris. The symbol «white lead»
(here named 3asfidäga) is based on the fact that lead in its raw form usually
contains silver that can be extracted. We saw white lead earlier as a name
for the stone in its whiteness (§ 121), which in al-Mä3 al-waraql is identified
with the perfect lead-copper.572 The symbol «water of lead» (mäJ abär)
571 Iron oxide hydrate (Fe203(H20). 572 Al-Mä}al-waraqî, p. 24.12, 55.20, 68.15.

[Page 249]
248 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
originates in the ancient belief mentioned before that the easily melting lead
contains water that was extracted from the first body, here named lead.573
All these symbols point to the divine water emerging from some body that
has been activated somehow by something.
«The Egyptian alum (sabb)» as the next symbol for the divine water
is a special substance. It is a milky-white stone that is still used in the Orient
today for purifying water (fig. 85). The traditional dyers use alum to
improve and fix their dyes. When dissolved, alum crystallizes out into a
beautiful transparent stone. These three features of alum—the ability to
Fig. 85: A piece of alum, crystallized out after having purified the water.
purify, to produce a good, permanent dye, and to undergo its own meta­
morphosis—help us to become more familiar with this symbol for the
divine water. Ibn Umail speaks at different places in his al-Mä: al-waraqï of
the alum of the dyers,574 while in the Mushaf as-suwar, Zosimos emphasizes
573
See e.g. Risäla Qalübatra malikat Samannüd, M. Ullmann, “Kleopatra”, p. 166, § 5:
«She [Cleopatra] asked: “Why do you name it [the stone] lead (abär)T They answered:
“Because after the synthesis it becomes moist water just as lead.”» For that reason, this metal
was connected to Osiris, who was imprisoned by his brother Seth in a coffin made of lead.
Later he was resurrected with the help of his wife Isis in the form of his son Horus, while he
himself remained in the underworld as “Master of all Liquids”. Lead can also be transformed
to ceruse, basic lead-carbonate, Pb(0H)2.2Pb(C03); see C. Priesner and K. Figala, Alchemie
Lexikon, s.v.Blei.
574
Al-Mä: al-waraqï, p. 57.23 and 62.24-25; in the Risäla Qalübatra Cleopatra asks the sages
why the stone is named alum. They answer: «Because alum is used to dye our work.» See
M. Ullmann, “Kleopatra”, p. 168, §19; see also E. O. von Lippmann, Entstehung, p. 414.

[Page 250]
Fire and Water
249
to his student Theosebeia the coagulating quality of the alum: «She said:
“This [divine] water, is it not the dew?” He said: “We named our water of
dew with many names, and I will explain it to you. But what prevented you
from knowing it is that we changed its name.” She said: “Maybe it is the
alum?” He said: “Yes. Do you not know that the alum is what prevents the
fugitives from escaping?”» The aspect of purifying with the help of alum
is also mentioned by Zosimos in the same text when he explains to
Theosebeia: «Then cook it until it absorbs the moisture and you see it turn­
ing black. Know that the more you continue cooking it, the more it turns
black. If you want the dye to become more precious than anything, put the
wool back into the alum and dye it with it (the alum) several times.»575
That means that the wool is whitened with the help of the alum.576 This text
describes the process of purification with the divine water, in other words
the further absorption of unconscious, shadowy elements with the help of
the insights that generate meaning embedded in the collected inner images.
The water of the vitriol (mäD al-zâg) is usually an old name for sul-
335
phates of heavy metals, mainly of copper and iron. The name vitriol goes
back to the Latin word vitrum for glass as both the crystals, copper-vitriol
and iron-vitriol are transparent and shining.577 In two variant readings of
our passage we find reference to the water of glass (mci: az-zugäg). In the
discussion between Cleopatra and the sages she asked them: «“Why is it
called vitriol (qalqcmd)T They answered: “This is because vitriol has
inside lightnings.”». The water of the vitriol seems to be another symbol
for the divine water that contains the sulphuric fire. It is solid and liquid in
the same way as the stone of the sages is solid and liquid. In its liquid form
it is the divine water.
The rest of this passage needs to be seen in the light of our expia- 336
nation of the great vision of Ibn Umail (see Part I). Here the divine water,
which is the union of the watery spirit and the fiery soul, is named «the
soap of wisdom». This means that insights gained from the images of the
unconscious can cleanse the first body of ego-centred impurities. The
575 Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 202b. 1-4, and fol. 91a.l0f. On fol. 208b. 13 f. he explains: «With the
composed water they make alumy.» On fol. 18la. 14 he identifies alum with the white sulphur.
The mystical purification of the sulphur by washing it with a solution of alum is
576
described in the third pillar of the cAin as-sanacaof Maqbül Ahmad. In H.E. Stapleton, "A
Persian Translation of the 11th Century Arabic Alchemical Treatise ‘cAin as-sanca wa caun
as-sinäca’ ” in: Memoirs, of the Asiatic Society of BengalVol. VIII, No. 7. p. 423.
577 This was described by Pliny the Elder (23-79) in his Naturalis Historiae; see C. Priesner
and K. Figala, Alchemie Lexikon,s.v.Vitriol.

[Page 251]
250 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
intense fire of passionate drivenness that is kept in the close proximity to
reality, yet at the same time held in suspension, allows the healing images
to appear that make it possible, in time, to capture and understand the
meaning of those fiery emotions. The two, fire and water, drive and drive-
control joined together are, indeed, «sufficient for your work» (see figs. 9
to 11 ).
Text: (63.8) Thus [it is] by this fire, I mean the soul, by which they
wash with the spirit and by which they bum the body till it becomes white.
That is the meaning of their saying: «By the water it is burned and by fire
it is washed». So know the water and the fire, with which they bum and
with which they wash. Both of them are those which they meant by the
water and the fire. (63.13)
The purification of the first body by fire and water, soul and spirit,
till it becomes white takes place in an unexpected way. «The fire by which
they wash» cleanses the body. The fiery emotions that finally enable one
to break free from a futile attachment, when a projection cannot be lived
concretely, needs to be cleansed by reflection. «The burning by the water»,
i.e. by the spirit, points on the other hand to the painful burning effect that
Fig. 86: The firelake in the Amduat. It is water for the blessed, but fire for the enemies
of the Sungod. (From Th. Abt and E. Hornung, Knowledge for the Afterlife.)

[Page 252]
Fire and Water 251
the regulating images of the unconscious, e.g. in dreams, have on the body.
They set limits on the craving for the eternal, the longing for identification
with the archetype, and thereby reduce the old ego to ashes. Both the burning
and the washing are in fact effects of one and the same divine water, the
union of soul and spirit, the fire-water. The paradoxical effect of this fire­
water is already mentioned in the Amduat, the earliest “Book of the
Afterlife” of the New Kingdom in Pharaonic Egypt (dating ca. 1500 BC).
There we read, in the lower register of the fifth hour, that the lake of fire is
fiery punishment for the enemies of the Sungod, while the blessed dead
drink cool water from the same lake. 578 The same feature of this water in
the Netherworld is also known from other Pharaonic Books of the Afterlife.
Text: (63.13) They named it the horn of a goat, and with [the names 339
of] all the alums, and with every salt, the bitterness of the salt, the oxymel,
the crown of victory, and the crown of the king. And they named it the
ashes extracted from the ashes, the crown of everything, and everything is
their stone. About these ashes the sage said: «When you take the crown
from my leg then put it on my head, and do not bring the ignorant close to
me.» By his statement «on my head» he meant that you dissolve these
ashes in the water by returning them to it [the water], because the water is
the head and the remaining sediment in the bottom of the vessel is the leg.
(65.7)
So far «the horn of a goat» as a symbol for the divine water has not 340
been traced either in other texts of Ibn Umail or in any of Ibn UmaiTs quoted
sources. The goat has a strong will of its own; for that this animal stands in
close relation to the devilish ‘unorthodox’ fantasies. Its horn could point to
the creativity which emerges from this powerful, autonomous, divine-
daemonic dynamism of the “unorthodox other”.
Here a new symbol for the divine water is the «ashes extracted from 34i
the ashes». In al-Mâ} al-waraqï, Ibn Umail explains this in greater detail:
«By all this they mean the nature which is in the water, and it is the soul
[= the fiery soul contained in the water of the spirit]. And the nature that is
in the body is the soul in it. The ashes of the ashes, which are the crown of
victory, rejoice in meeting their sister who is the supreme soul and spirit. The
nature which is the lower body rejoices in the heavenly nature because it
is similar to it, and as it is from it, it holds it, clings to it, and wins over
Th. Abt and E. Homung, Knowledge of the Afterlife, p. 72.
578

[Page 253]
252 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
it.»579 From this we can conclude that the «ashes of the ashes» are a
symbol of the purified, refined essence of the «lower body» that is the
adept him- or herself. It is that unique individual reality of the adept about
which he or she has become conscious, and that has now been accepted as
just-so fact. This insight has been extracted from the whitened first body
that is called the ashes. These «ashes of the ashes», which in our passage
from the Hall ar-rumuz are related to «the remaining sediment in the bottom
of the vessel that is the leg», are then reunited with the divine water.580 This
causes a coagulation of the divine water in the second body.
Psychologically this means the final reconciliation of the lower with the
upper realm, the manifestation of the bodily urges with its correlated images.
This corresponds to the experience of the living emanation of Allah that has
entered into the reality of the unique life of the adept. C. G. Jung has
interpreted this coniunctio of the upper and the lower «... as the union of
a consciousness (spirit), differentiated by self-knowledge with a spiritual
abstraction from previously unconscious contents. One could also regard
the latter as a quintessence of fantasy-images that enter consciousness
either spontaneously or through active imagination and, in their totality,
represent a moral or intellectual viewpoint contrasting with, or compensating
for that of consciousness. To begin with, however, these images are anything
but ‘moral’ or ‘intellectual’; they are more or less concrete visualisations
Al-Mâ3 al-waraqï, p. 60.21-24. The text continues: «Its winning over it is its controlling
579
it, and its keeping it with it in its subtle body, which is operated on and refined until it
becomes subtle-spiritual like it. Then at that time the colours appear in the body through the
soul which is in the body. Thus the earth here dyed the water with the soul that is in it, and
the water dyed the earth with the soul that is in it. So each of the two dyed the other, the dyer
and the dyed become one dye that dyes the silver of the people. The water is the dyer, and the
earth is the dyed. In the earth there is also water that dyed the water, and it is the soul that is
in it [the earth] which I have named the sulphurs of the earth.» Al-Mâ: al-waraqï, p. 60.24-61.1.
580 This union of the upper sun with the lower earth and the ashes of the ashes are also
described again later in al-Mä: al-waraqr. «So when they married, I mean the subtle high one
and the subtle part of the thick earth, the subtle high is male at this point. The air is likewise
male, and the two of them come from one thing. This is the marriage of the earth, the brother
with his sister, without whom he can do nothing, just as she can do nothing without him and
the ashes of the ashes. Then the result appeared from them which is the baby, the purple dye,
the greatest, generous, high-ranking, and honourable king, who is like these, his parents, even
more refined and subtle, as the mother is moon-like luminous and the father is sun-like exalted
luminous.» This text describes the conjunction of father sun, the upper male, and mother
moon, which is also the second body, the purified psyche of the adept on earth. Quoted from
the al-Mâ: al-waraqï. p. 100.10-14. The relation to the Tabula Smaragdina is obvious.

[Page 254]
Fire and Water 253
that first have to be interpreted.»581 This work of interpretation is what has
been called by Ibn Umail «distilling soul and spirit out of the body», i.e. to
ponder over the meaning of the different urges and impulses coming from the
bodily realm. These are always experienced first in a projected form as
strong emotions of either attraction or repulsion. In time, this lowest will
turn out to be what he calls «the crown of victory» or «the crown of the
king»: «When you take the crown from my leg, then put it on my head, and
do not bring the ignorant close to me.» The divine value of the lowest is
not understandable to the ignorant and therefore despised by them. As
Marie-Louise von Franz has worked out in her commentary, Ibn Umail
speaks here of the union, and by that the reconciliation of the whole individual
pattern of the adept, including its shadow with the upper water, with Allah.
Text: (65.8) And they also named their water the green woodpecker, 342
which is a multi-coloured bird. It is found in the harbours and in the
countries where there is much rain, and it is of ill omen. (And they named
it] the urine of boys, the blood of gazelles, the urine of the new-borns, the
urine of cows, the urine of asses and the urine of the bitch. They compare
it with urine for the way it descends from [a thing like] the urethra of the
alembic because they said about this water of theirs: «Raise [it] with the
one that has a urethra». (67.3)
The green woodpecker as a name for the divine water might have 343
been inspired by the hoopoe, which has a similar long beak. The hoopoe is
the guiding bird in Farid ud-DTn c Attar’s Mantiq at-taïr (Conference of
the Birds), a story that probably predates cAttär (1145-ca. 1221 ), since
Ibn Umail also compared the stone to the meeting place of the birds, as we
saw earlier.582 The experience of seeing a green woodpecker is quite
numinous: There it is, sitting on a tree, hardly noticeable, maybe only
signalling its presence with a loud, startling shrieking noise. But when it
flies away, spreading its wings, it reveals a wonderful green colour. The
notion that this green woodpecker should be a bad omen seems to me to be a
misunderstanding of an early copyist, as the green colour points to the holy
colour of the Muslims, and to the life-giving quality of the divine water.583
581 C. G. Jung, Mysr. coni., § 736.
582 See Hall ar-mmüz, p. 33.19.
583 When I was writing my commentary on this passage, I noticed on the tree outside where I
was working a shrieking noise, and when I looked out of the window I saw for the first time in
my life a beautiful green woodpecker flying off. I found it hard to imagine this to be a bad omen.

[Page 255]
254 Commentary on the Hall ar-nimuz
?44 The different urines as names for the divine water are for Ibn Umail
the clarified part that is raised up above the body, while the dry sediment
is called the dung on which the urine is returned as divine water.584 In the
dialogue of Cleopatra with the sages, she asks them why the honoured
stone is named urine (haul). They answer: «Because all the dyers create
their precious colours with the help of urine.»585 For the dyers, urine had a
similar function as alum, which has already been pointed out by Zosimos.586
345 Text: (67.3) And they named it the saliva of the sun, the saliva of
the dragon, the poison of the vipers, the air, the spirit of the air, and the
woman scaring the enemy. They meant by that the blackness. (67.5)
346 «The saliva of the sun» as a symbol for the divine water points
again to its paradoxical dry-moist nature, while «the saliva of the dragon»,
and «the poison of the vipers» point to its dark, poisonous side. «The air»,
and «the spirit of the air» remind us that the union of the vapour and the
smoke takes place in the air, and therefore this divine water is, indeed, «the
spirit of the air». Finally «the woman scaring the enemy» is again that
aspect of the divine water that brings the frightening second blackness.
What we see illustrated by these symbols is the main feature of alchemy,
namely that in their perplexing, paradoxical union of the opposites the
collectively rejected devilish, hideous side of the self is never excluded.
347 Text: (67.5) And they named it the water of the twelve signs of the
zodiac, and they carried out in them the nine soakings. Thus they rendered
them into houses for the seven planets, meaning the body and the seven
soakings. Thus they named the nine soakings with the [names of the] seven
planets, and the son of the year with yellow reddishness, and the son of the
six bodies, which are the six parts isolated from the water for the reddening.
(67.11)
348 The zodiac signs as well as the seven planets are mentioned in al-
MäJ al-waraqî. together with a helpful explanation: «This remedy, which
is their elixir, is only made complete out of various things, according to this
584 Al-Mä’ al-waraqî, p. 96.1-3: «[...] it is that which some of the sages named excrement
and dung, and they named its clarified part urine. When they took away its clarified part,
which are the souls and the spirits, they named this clarified part urine, while they named its
sediment excrement and dung.»
585 M. Ullmann, "Kleopatra”, p. 169.
586 Mushaf as-suwar, fol. 94b.5-6.

[Page 256]
Fire and Water 255
accounting and the many days. By that they also mean what they symbolized
by mentioning the seven planets and the twelve zodiac signs and also their
natures and their colours, and all that is in them. An analogy of this is the
grain of wheat. It only becomes a seed in its plantation with the help of
various things, although it is one thing. The various things are its operation
from the many days and nights, and the dew, the sun, the ploughing, the
harvesting, the threshing, the winnowing, and the number of people and
beasts who work on it, and the purification, milling and sieving. Thus this
grain is not ready until it has been milled, and become dough, and been
fermented, and made into baked bread, involving various things as I made
clear to you in this account. Their stone is like that. It only becomes an
elixir of various things, like its many days and nights and hours, and what
happens in that time as we described its changes in the transformation and
the operation, the manifoldness of which cannot be counted.»587 This
quote shows that the divine water, which is the soul and the spirit extracted
from the first body, does not become complete, i.e. an elixir, except when
it is connected to the zodiac, or more precisely to the course of the twelve
signs through which the sun and the planets pass. (Sun and Moon were
considered at that time to be planets, like Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter
and Saturn.) This connection of the divine water with the reality of time
and space belongs to the second part of the work, which is when the
extracted insights into the symbolism of the unconscious are to be con­
fronted and reunited with the reality of the adept’s individual life. This is a
long process in the course of time, compared by Ibn Umail with the long
journey from the grain to the nourishing bread.
Text: (67.12) And they named their water the water of the heaven, 349
the solution of the wind, the airy water, the milk of rose-water, the fiery
sword, the rust of gold, and the pyrite, whose explanation is the sulphur
water. And it is the first pyrite, while the second pyrite is the sediment and
the master of the waters. (69.3)
Their water is what has been extracted from the first body, which is 350
then in the upper part of the vessel and hence is the «water of heaven», etc.
«The milk of the rose-water» needs further amplification. This symbol has
so far only been mentioned in this text of Ibn Umail, but sources in Greek
alchemy shed some light on it. In a text of Nikephoros we read of rose oil
587 Al-Mä’ al-waraqi, p. 30.8-16.

[Page 257]
256 Commentary on the Hall ar-rumuz
(rodostagma), which can be obtained from distilling rose water.588 The
milk of the rose water is most probably this rose-oil, and this would point
to the eros-quality of the divine water.589 The fiery sword refers to the exact
opposite of this eros-quality, namely its ability to bring about clear dis­
crimination. For that we only have to think of the incredible precise ability
of dreams to point out our shadow qualities that are usually only seen in
our neighbour. The divine water is both water and fire, connecting and
separating. The other symbols mentioned here we have seen earlier.
Text: (69.3) Maria the sage named it the fleece of wool and the
woollen dress. She also said: «Put the fleece of wool in the woollen bag.»
And she said «Dye the fleece of wool.» They named the water the wool and
they named the body the firewood of the wool. [They named] the wool the
water because it accepts the dyes, and it rises upon the body, and from the
body it came out. And they named it the fur of rabbits because fur and wool
rise from the body upon the surface of the body just as their water rises up
from their body. Thus it is also like feathers and hair in their rising up from
the body upon the body. Therefore it is compared with both. In another
interpretation. Maria named it the talcous kohl and the burnt copper rising
up from the vapour. The burning is the dissolution with the water. It is also
the urine of a goat, the milk of a goat, the milk of a sheep, the water of
ashes, sour vinegar, the water of lemon and the acid of lemon and every
beverage. (71.7)
«Wool» (siif), «fur» (yvabar, esp. fur of camel and goats), and
«feathers» (risa) all grow out of the first body that remains as sediment in
the bottom of the vessel. All these symbols guide us to some specific
quality of the divine water. Wool, for instance, is well-known for its ability to
be dyed, and the final dye of wool in alchemy is always red.590 «Put the
fleece of wool in the woollen bag» and then «dye the fleece of wool»,
588 M. Berthelot, Alch. grecs IV, and 8; see also E. O. von Lippmann, Entstehung I.
XX.2,
p. 110.
589 This hypothesis is supported by a text from Zosimos, who says: «This is because when
the lime [which is equal here in this text to the ashes] is washed seven times with the oil of
the rose, it will be beneficial to the burnt dry bodies. Know that when the composition is burnt
at the beginning, and is put back seven times, it benefits from that. You put it back, in order
that its burning becomes complete, and in order that the subtle part of the moisture enters into
the interior of the subtle part of the ashes (= burnt body).» Majàiïh as-san(a, fol. 80b.
17-21.
590 In the ritual dance of the whirling dervishes of the Mevlevi Sufi order (who follows
Galâl ad-DIn Muhammad Rümï) in Pera, Istanbul, the master sits on a red woollen fleece.

[Page 258]
Fire and Water 257
speak again of a container (the bag) that needs to receive the dyed fleece
of wool, which is now dyed red. The whole turns again into a symbol of
the stone in its final stage: the white woollen bag outside and the red dyed
